# Spec: dataplane-compatibility-guard

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Status | ready |
| Scope | config |
| Priority | P1 |
| Release reason | Ze accepts configurations whose features program a data plane other than the one the interfaces run on. The config commits and the feature then does nothing, or fails when it is applied. Accepted but ineffective configuration is incorrect shipped behavior. |
| RFC gaps | - |
| Depends | `plan/release/fixes/spec-firewall-vpp-apply-before-connect.md` (Phase 6 only: what the static named-table backend does while VPP is not yet connected); `plan/release/fixes/spec-explicit-plugin-and-attach-config.md` (coordination, Q-2): that spec owns how a command with no daemon obtains an external program's declaration (query mode, its A-Q2 and P-1) and the `bus { endpoint }` grammar that replaces the `plugin` blocks; this spec owns `ze config validate` reading the `--plugin` arguments the daemon takes (AC-3), and needs nothing from that spec for a compiled-in plugin, whose declaration is in the registry |
| Phase | - |
| Handoff | - |
| Updated | 2026-11-23 |

Recovery after compaction: `.claude/rules/post-compaction.md`.

## Task

Thomas asked (voice-transcribed, read for meaning) for a page that says which
features work on VPP and on the kernel, then for the information to live in the
code "to prevent incompatible configuration combinations", and then: "yes please
make a spec for that work".

Ze has four independent data-plane selections: `interface backend`,
`firewall backend`, `traffic control backend` and the `fib` blocks. Each is
checked against the features under it. Nothing compares them with each other,
and features with no backend leaf (IPsec, L2TP, flow export sampling, policy
routing, GTSM) are never compared with `interface backend`. Ten combinations
are accepted today and then misbehave (table in Current Behavior, rows A to H,
J and K).

The goal: each feature that writes forwarding state declares the data plane it
programs, in its own package. The config component derives one verdict from
those declarations and the selected data plane. A mismatch is refused at
commit, at daemon start, at reload and by `ze config validate`, and reported by
`ze doctor`, with the same words and the line that fixes it: one verdict, the
same at every door (C-5). The hand-written page
`docs/guide/dataplane-features.md` is replaced by a page generated from the same
declarations.

Thomas decided the first product choice, C-1, on 2026-11-23 (voice-transcribed):
"should Ze refuse a kernel-only feature when the interfaces run on VPP: the
commit should fail". Reading taken: refuse outright, with no host-side
exception.

He answered the four other choices on 2026-11-23 (voice-transcribed): "C-2 b /
C-3 a / C-4 a / C-5 we should not refuse to start the deamon if the issue is
that something will not be configured but we should refuse to commit any more
changed until it is fixed". He then replaced his answer to C-5: "You know what.
Refuse invalid configuration at boot", and gave the reason: "simpler".
He then answered the two questions the design judge's refusal raised and the
two pending confirmations, on 2026-11-23 (voice-transcribed): "q1 b - so we can
consider mix setup when we add vrf / q2 I replied elsewhere another agent
should have written it down, it should start the plugin to get the yang / p1 b
atm / p2 delete". "Choices for Thomas" records each answer with the reading
taken (C-1 to C-5, Q-1, Q-2, P-1, P-2). AC-3, AC-5, AC-14 to AC-17 state the
outcome of each, and the three decision rows of
`docs/architecture/core-design.md` carry them.

After the design was approved, Thomas asked (voice-transcribed): "should tc and
netfilter be renamed to kernel? so we have vpp and kernel?", and, once the main
session recommended it, "please do it". Reading taken (choice N-1): every
data-plane selector names the plane it programs. `interface backend`,
`firewall backend` and `traffic control backend` take `kernel` where they took
`netlink`, `nft` and `tc` ("netfilter" is read as `nft`, the nftables
backend), each defaults to `kernel`, and `vpp` is unchanged; the `fib` blocks
already say `kernel` and `vpp`. The same word is the plane every registration
declares and the value every `ze:backend` annotation names. Go package names
(`firewall/nft`, `traffic/netlink`, `iface/netlink`) stay. Ze is pre-release, so
no alias accepts the old values and no conversion code is written
(`ai/rules/no-layering.md`; `ai/META-DECISIONS.md`, "Ze is pre-release"): every
repository user is converted in the same work, and a configuration that still
names an old value is refused with the fix that names `kernel` (AC-19). The
accepted cost: a second implementation on the same plane, such as eBPF, takes a
selector value of its own when it exists. The fourth decision row of
`docs/architecture/core-design.md` carries it.

## Required Reading

### Architecture Docs
- [ ] `ai/doors/system.md` - process boundary, registration, exact or refuse
  → Constraint: the engine and config code name no feature, plugin or config path; each feature declares from its own `init()`.
  → Constraint: "A backend that cannot apply a config exactly refuses it at verify."
- [ ] `ai/doors/forwarding.md` - backends, FIB writers, ownership
  → Decision: one writer per data plane; a FIB plugin declares `ProgramsFIB` and `DataPlane`, a producer declares `NeedsDataPlane`.
  → Constraint: feature support is declared with `ze:backend` and checked by `config.validateBackendFeatures`; a semantic limit goes in a backend verifier.
  → Constraint: "The firewall table registry is process-local." Every table producer runs in process, so a producer may register a config table builder with the firewall component from its own `init()`, as it already calls `RegisterTables` in process at apply.
- [ ] `ai/doors/config-yang.md` - where validation runs
  → Constraint: `ze config validate` never calls a live `OnConfigVerify`; a rule that lives only there passes offline and fails at start. The verdict MUST run in the doors that boot, reload and validate share.
  → Constraint: "A root that a reload adds has no running participant." The verdict reads the tree and the registry, never a running plugin.
- [ ] `ai/doors/plugin-infra.md` - registry is a leaf, engine never enumerates plugins
  → Constraint: a new declaration is a `registry.Registration` field or a registry function a package calls from `init()`; the registry package imports no plugin.
- [ ] `ai/doors/system-plugins.md`, `ai/doors/access-security.md` - static, firewall backends, IKE, L2TP
  → Constraint: `ike/dataplane` stays ungated because OSPF uses it for RFC 4552; the IPsec plane declaration belongs to the IKE engine registration, never to that shared seam.
  → Constraint: a parser that answers nil for "no section" and for "nothing to apply" hides a removal; each in-use predicate reads presence in the tree explicitly.
  → Constraint: L2TP is a subsystem with no `registry.Registration` (`Engine.RegisterSubsystem`); its declaration needs a registry function it calls from `init()`, as it already calls `config.RegisterTreeValidator`.
- [ ] `ai/doors/le-tooling.md` - the doc generator and the site manifest
  → Constraint: `le` may link the composition root, so the page generator reads the same registry `ze` fills; a generated page is written and compared by one program.
- [ ] `docs/architecture/core-design.md`, sections 14, 14a, 14b - backend split and the commit-time gate
  → Decision: an absent `ze:backend` annotation means unrestricted; the narrowest annotation wins.
  → Constraint: the gate is called today from each component's `OnConfigure` and from the parse its `OnConfigVerify` and `InProcessConfigVerifier` share; `ze config validate` reaches it only through `config.VerifyPluginConfig` (section 14a, corrected 2026-11-23 by this spec's author: the page named a gate loop in `cmd_validate.go` that does not exist).
- [ ] `docs/features/interfaces.md`, "Outer-header TTL" - what the gate reads
  → Constraint: "the gate reads what the operator wrote and the schema default is materialized after it". The engine walk runs on the tree as written, before any default is materialized, so a vpp tunnel that names no `ttl` still commits.
- [ ] `docs/architecture/config/yang-config-design.md`, "Layer 3: Plugin config verifiers" - where an in-process verifier runs
  → Constraint: an `InProcessConfigVerifier` "does not run at daemon startup". A producer check that must refuse at start lives in an original-tree validator (`config.RegisterTreeValidator`, run by `ValidateCustomSections` in `LoadConfig`), never in a plugin verifier alone.
  → Constraint: an `InProcessConfigVerifier` "receives its own config subtree". A rule that compares a leaf of one root with a leaf of another root (the WireGuard interface and `vpp plugins wireguard`, case B) cannot live in either root's verifier; it lives in a whole-tree check.
- [ ] `docs/architecture/firewall/table-ownership-and-shutdown-flush.md` - who registers firewall tables
  → Constraint: every caller of `firewall.RegisterTables` is a firewall backend user; AC-3 and AC-8 name each one and its outcome (the producer table under Acceptance Criteria).
- [ ] `ai/rules/user-facing-errors.md` - refusal shape
  → Constraint: a refusal is an `opererr` error: problem, why, and the exact fix alone on the last line; it carries a doctor code.
- [ ] `ai/META-DECISIONS.md`, "CLI, config, and daemon surfaces" - never widen
  → Constraint: where the operator wrote two things that disagree, the commit is refused with the line that completes it.
- [ ] `plan/release/fixes/spec-explicit-plugin-and-attach-config.md`, "Offline validation asks the program", its A-Q2 and its P-1; `docs/architecture/plugin/plugin-system.md`, the external-plugin rule table ("With no daemon running, no command starts it"); `ai/doors/config-yang.md`, "Who depends on it from outside" - how a command with no daemon learns a plugin's declarations
  → Decision: Thomas, 2026-11-23 (Q-2, voice-transcribed): "it should start the plugin to get the yang". That spec owns the query-mode route for an external program and the reversal of the `plugin-system.md` ruling; this spec does not repeat it.
  → Constraint: a compiled-in plugin's declarations are in the registry, so `ze config validate` needs no program run to judge one named with `--plugin`.

**Key insights:**
- The per-node fact already has one declaration (`ze:backend`); the per-feature and per-backend data plane has none.
- `registry.ConfigNeed` and `registry.DataPlaneWriter` are the two shapes to copy: a declaration on the registration, evaluated by the config component over the parsed tree, with one judgment shared by boot, reload and doctor.
- `config.RegisterTreeValidator` is the existing whole-tree boundary, and the PPPoE backend gate already uses it to compare a feature with `interface backend`, reading the schema default.
- `kernelcap.Capability` already pairs a requirement with an `InUse(tree)` predicate declared by the owning package.

**Source files read:**
- [ ] `internal/component/config/backend_gate.go` - `validateBackendFeatures` walks one root against one backend name; `formatBackendError` renders the path, the backend and the supporting list. A schema or root it cannot find answers no error.
- [ ] `internal/component/iface/register.go`, `internal/component/firewall/engine.go`, `internal/component/traffic/register.go`, `internal/plugins/container/network/backend.go` - the four callers of `config.ValidateBackendFeaturesJSON` (confirmed by search: no other caller); each caches the schema and passes its own root and backend. iface, firewall and traffic call it from `OnConfigure` and from the parse their `OnConfigVerify` and `InProcessConfigVerifier` share (`parseAndVerifyIfaceSections`, `parseAndVerifyFirewallSections`, `parseAndVerifyTrafficSections`). The container plugin calls it from `checkBackends` with the interface backend, reading both backend leaves with hardcoded defaults (`interfaceBackendDefault`, `firewallBackendDefault`).
- [ ] `internal/component/config/cli/cmd_validate.go` - `runValidationUnanswered` holds no backend gate loop. It runs `config.ValidateCustomSections`, `kernelcap.Refuse` and `config.VerifyPluginConfig`, and reaches the gate only through the three components' in-process verifiers.
- [ ] `internal/component/config/plugin_verify.go` - `VerifyPluginConfig` (`ze config validate`, editor pre-commit) and `VerifyPluginConfigTransition` (editor commit) both call `verifyTree`, the one function of this door that holds the candidate `*Tree`: it calls `ExtractPluginsFromTree` for the candidate's plugin selection and runs `refuseUnresolvableDependencies` over the tree, after `VerifyPluginConfigMapTransition`, which receives only the lowered map (`ToPluginMap`) and runs `refuseAbsentOptionalProviders` and every registration's `InProcessConfigVerifier`. `refuseUnresolvableDependencies` says what it does not cover: the FIB writer boot adds for the configured data plane, "because the data plane is read by the iface component, which config does not import".
- [ ] `internal/component/config/loader.go` - `loadConfig` (boot, SIGHUP reload) runs `refuseInvalidCustomSections`, then `refuseAbsentOptionalProviders`, then `RefuseRedistributeRules`, before any plugin starts.

Start calls it once, and `RecoverConfig` calls it for each rollback version when the stored configuration comes from a newer release. Every reload (SIGHUP, `request reload`, and each commit the daemon authors) reads its candidate through `diskConfigLoaders`, which calls it too; a refusal there ends the reload before `ReloadConfig`, with the running configuration still serving. `judgeConfigBeforeBoot` judges a pushed candidate with it. `lowerForPlugins` hands plugins `ActiveConfigMap` of the running map.

The editor calls `sectionErrors` (`ValidateCustomSections`), `VerifyPluginConfig` and `VerifyPluginConfigTransition`. The API commit calls `ValidateContent`, which runs the body of `ze config validate`, then `VerifyPluginConfigContentTransition`; the config upload calls `ValidateContent`. Each judges the candidate alone and holds no state of the running daemon.
- [ ] The doors that judge a candidate, each traced to where it is built (2026-11-23), and what each holds of the command line. Inside a running daemon: the SSH editor (`internal/component/cli/model.go` builds `NewConfigValidator()`, which takes no argument); the web editor commit (`internal/component/web/editor_commit_window.go` `validateTransition`, the same constructor); the web editor factory and the web upload (`cmd/ze/hub/service_web.go` hands both `zeconfigcmd.ValidateContent`); the REST and gRPC config sessions (`cmd/ze/hub/api.go` `buildAPIShared` sets `configValidationHook`, built from `apiBuildInputs` in `cmd/ze/hub/api_infra.go`, which has no plugin field); gNMI Set (`cmd/ze/hub/service_gnmi.go` `buildGNMISessionManager` sets the same hook); the SSH config upload (`cmd/ze/hub/config_upload.go` `configMutationOwner.Upload`, bound by `sessionMutations.bind` in `cmd/ze/hub/main.go`); the managed client's push handler (`cmd/ze/ze_core_start.go`, the `managed.Handler` `Validate` closure, which calls `config.ReadConfig` with `nil` command-line plugins and so reaches `loadConfig`, not `verifyTree`). With no daemon: `ze config validate`, and the offline editing sessions (`internal/component/config/cli/artifact_session.go`, `internal/component/config/cli/config_detached.go`, `internal/component/config/artifact/edit.go`), which call `ValidateContent` or build the same validator. Every one of them but the push handler reaches `verifyTree`: through `ValidateContent` (`runValidationUnanswered`, then `VerifyPluginConfig`), through `VerifyPluginConfigContentTransition`, or through the validator's own `VerifyPluginConfig` and `VerifyPluginConfigTransition` calls. None takes or holds a plugin list: in `runYANGConfig` only the start call and the reload loaders hold `plugins`. One door judges in another process: `ze config edit` with no daemon running (`runStoredEditor` in `internal/component/config/cli/cmd_edit.go`) starts one through `startEphemeralDaemon`, whose argv is `<exe> start [--web <port>] [--insecure-web] <path>`, and opens an SSH editor session on it. That daemon's dispatcher parses its own argv, which names no plugin, so it records none: today `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall config edit` commits, through the ephemeral daemon's SSH editor, a candidate that `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall start` and `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall config validate` refuse once the verdict exists.
- [ ] `internal/component/config/cli/register.go`, `internal/component/config/cli/main.go` - the `config` root handler discards its `registry.RuntimeContext` and calls `Run(args)`; `Run`, `RunWithStorage` and every entry of `subcommandHandlers` take the arguments alone.
- [ ] `cmd/ze/ze_core_dispatch.go` `zeDispatch`, `cmd/ze/ze_core_start.go` `cmdStart`, `cmdStartManaged`, `cmd/ze/setup_dispatch.go` - `--plugin` is parsed in one place, into `zeFlags.plugins`, before `zeDispatch` runs any verb. Every daemon start is handed that slice (`cmdStart`, `cmdStartManaged`, the bare-file form), `newZeRuntimeContext` copies it for the root handlers, and the setup flavor starts the daemon with none.
- [ ] `cmd/ze/pushed_config.go` `judgeConfigBeforeBoot` - it holds `plugins` and hands them to `config.FirstPass`, then calls `config.LoadConfig` with `nil` in their place, so a pushed candidate is parsed and judged without the plugins the boot after it starts with.
- [ ] `internal/component/doctor/doctor.go` `runChecks` - `ze doctor` reads the stored file through `config.ReadConfig` with `nil` command-line plugins.
- [ ] `internal/component/config/declared_schemas.go` `declaredStep`, `RecordStartedPlugins`, `RecordDaemonUnanswered`, `publishDeclaredSchemas` - a process-wide record in the config component, guarded by one mutex and documented safe for concurrent use. The daemon writes the external plugins it started once, at boot, before it serves a command (`runYANGConfig`, before `LoadConfig`); a command with no daemon writes why none answered. Every later `SchemaWithDeclaredPlugins` and `PublishDeclaredSchemas` of the process reads it, whichever door called, with no parameter threaded to the door. It does not hold the `--plugin` list.
- [ ] `internal/component/config/loader.go` `MergeCliPlugins`, `loadConfig`, `internal/component/config/plugin_verify.go` `ParseTreeForValidation` - `MergeCliPlugins` resolves each command-line name with `plugin.ResolvePlugin` and refuses one that does not resolve, or that resolves to `auto`, so a merge can fail. `loadConfig` joins the module text of the command-line plugins before it parses (`plugin.CollectPluginYANG`); `ParseTreeForValidation`, the parse of the validate and commit doors, joins none.
- [ ] `internal/chaos/inprocess/runner.go` - the `le chaos run` in-process simulation calls the BGP loader's `LoadReactorWithPluginsStandalone`, which hands `config.LoadConfig` the command-line plugins `["ze.bgp-rs"]`, in a process the `ze` dispatcher never ran: no record of command-line plugins exists there, and the parameter is the only holder of the list.
- [ ] `internal/component/iface/register.go` - the registration declares `ConfigRoots` `interface` and `ConfigReads` `class-of-service`, so `verifyIfaceConfig`, its `InProcessConfigVerifier`, is handed those two roots and never the `vpp` root.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/fib/kernel/register.go` - `fib-kernel` owns the root `fib/kernel` and declares `ProgramsFIB` and `DataPlane: "netlink"`.
- [ ] `internal/component/plugin/server/startup_autoload.go` `fibPluginChosen` - a configured, running or candidate plugin that declares `ProgramsFIB` stops `appendDataPlaneWriter` from adding the writer of the configured data plane. With a `fib kernel` block and no `fib vpp` block under `interface backend vpp`, no VPP writer is loaded.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/anomaly/shape/register.go` - a further table producer (`firewall` dependency), tables built by its responder at run time.
- [ ] `internal/component/gtsm/gtsm.go`, `internal/component/gtsm/route_linux.go`, `internal/component/gtsm/register.go` - `SetPeers`, called by the BGP reactor (`publishGTSMKernelState`) for every peer whose `ttl` container sets `max`, `set` or `min`, installs kernel host routes carrying `RTAX_HOPLIMIT` (`applyHopLimitRoutes`, `installHopLimitRoute`) and publishes the GTSM drop table through `firewall.RegisterTables` and `ApplyAll` (`publishFilter`, `FilterTables`). It has no `registry.Registration`; `register.go` registers only a doctor check. Nothing it writes reaches VPP.
- [ ] `internal/component/firewall/plugins/irr/irr.go`, `internal/component/firewall/plugins/irr/register.go`, `internal/component/firewall/plugins/domain/domain.go`, `internal/component/firewall/plugins/domain/register.go` - both own the `firewall` root; `applyTables` builds tables with named sets (IRR AS-SET prefixes, resolved domain addresses) and registers them. The set contents arrive at run time; the set shape is fixed by the configuration. A firewall term that names such a set is a `MatchInSet` in the firewall's own table, which the VPP verifier refuses at commit (`verify.go`, the `MatchInSet` case). IRR interface bindings (`parseIRRConfig`, `firewall irr interface`) build a table of their own (`buildIfaceTables`), which the VPP verifier refuses only in `ApplyAll` (`verifyTable`: named sets). The firewall's `OnConfigure` runs `parseConfigSections`, not the verifier, so at daemon start every one of these refusals surfaces as an apply failure, which stops the startup tier.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/vrrp/acceptfilter.go`, `internal/plugins/vrrp/backupfilter.go`, `internal/plugins/vrrp/ownerfilter.go`, `internal/component/ike/engine/source_guard.go` - further `RegisterTables` callers; each runs only with a feature already refused under `interface backend vpp` (VRRP by its `ze:backend "netlink"` annotation, the source guard with IPsec by AC-5).
- [ ] `internal/plugins/iface/netlink/register_tables.go`, `internal/plugins/iface/netlink/rpf_linux.go`, `internal/plugins/container/network/apply.go` - not registry producers: the netlink interface backend writes the rpf table over netlink and only reserves its name; the container plugin sends its tables through the `FirewallTablesReplace` RPC, and its network list is already refused under `vpp` by its annotation.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/iface/vpp/doctor.go` - the `vpp-wireguard-plugin` check, reporting `doctor-vpp-wireguard` at error severity.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/firewall/vpp/backend_linux.go`, `internal/plugins/traffic/vpp/backend_linux.go` - `Apply` waits for a VPP connector, then `dumpInterfaces` lists VPP's interfaces by VPP name.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/vrrp/groups.go`, `internal/plugins/vrrp/doctor.go` - `validateGroups` refuses VRRP under `vpp` in Go; the VRRP YANG sits under the interface units and carries `ze:backend "netlink"`, so the interface gate already refuses it.
- [ ] `internal/component/plugin/doctor/register_doctor_codes.go` - `doctor-plugin-no-dataplane-writer`.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/static/register_doctor_codes.go` - the static plugin's doctor codes; none covers a VPP channel failure.
- [ ] `internal/le/doc/yangcontract/drift.go`, `internal/le/site/docsmanifest.go` - `checkPipeOperatorReference` and `writeGenerated`, the generated-page route; the manifest row of `guide/dataplane-features.md` (an uncommitted page and row, added this session).

**Accepted today, then misbehaves:**

Each configuration below is written with the selector values of N-1: `kernel`
stands for today's `netlink` (interface), `nft` (firewall) and `tc` (traffic
control). The behavior described is today's.

| ID | Configuration | Producer | What happens |
|----|---------------|----------|--------------|
| A | `fib vpp` with `interface backend kernel` | `iface.ResolveVPPIndex`, called from `toFibPath` in `internal/plugins/fib/vpp/backend.go` | Commit succeeds. Each route with an interface next hop fails when it is installed; a gateway-only route is programmed into a VPP the interfaces do not run on. |
| B | `interface backend vpp` with a `wireguard` interface and `vpp plugins wireguard` not true | `checkVPPWireguardPlugin` in `internal/plugins/iface/vpp/doctor.go` | Commit succeeds. Only `ze doctor` reports it. The interface apply fails. |
| C | `interface backend vpp` with `firewall backend kernel` or `traffic control backend kernel` (the defaults), or a table producer with no `firewall` block | `validateBackendGate` in `internal/component/firewall/engine.go` and `internal/component/traffic/register.go` reads its own backend only; `ApplyAll` autoloads the OS default backend for producer tables | Commit succeeds. Rules and qdiscs land in the kernel. |
| D | `firewall backend vpp` or `traffic control backend vpp` with `interface backend kernel` | `dumpInterfaces` in `internal/plugins/firewall/vpp/backend_linux.go` and `internal/plugins/traffic/vpp/backend_linux.go` lists VPP's interfaces | Commit succeeds. The VPP backend is asked for interfaces the kernel owns. |
| E | IPsec, L2TP, flow export sampling or policy routing with `interface backend vpp` | IPsec: `ikeDataplaneName` loads `xfrm`. L2TP: `prepareKernelBackend` opens the OS default backend on purpose. Sampling: `SetupSampling` takes the index `iface.Resolve` answers, which under `vpp` is a VPP `sw_if_index`, and installs a kernel tc filter on it. Policy routing: kernel `ip rule` entries and the `ze_pr` table | Commit succeeds. State is programmed in the kernel; sampling programs a kernel filter on whatever link carries VPP's index number. |
| F | `snat` or `masquerade` with a source address match under `firewall backend vpp` | `verifyNATSourceMatches`: "Accepted but informational" | Commit succeeds. The match is not applied. |
| G | A static route in a named table | `newStaticBackend`, `newVPPStaticBackend`, called once by `runStaticPlugin` | The backend is VPP when a VPP connector exists as the plugin starts, the kernel otherwise, with a WARN when the channel fails to open. A static plugin run as an external process never sees a connector. The configuration does not decide. |
| H | A control-plane policing table under `firewall backend vpp` | `applyCoppPolicy` registers it; the verifier captured in `ApplyAll` judges it after the commit | The commit succeeds, then the VPP verifier refuses the table at apply: its jump to a non-base chain and its named sets are refused by `verifyChain` and `verifyTable`. |
| J | A BGP peer or group with `ttl max`, `ttl set` or `ttl min` under `interface backend vpp` | `gtsm.SetPeers`: `installHopLimitRoute` writes kernel host routes, `publishFilter` registers the GTSM drop table and `ApplyAll` loads the OS default backend when no `firewall` block loaded one | Commit succeeds. Kernel routes and a kernel nft table are programmed; VPP gets nothing |
| K | An IRR interface binding (`firewall irr interface <name> <as-set>`) under `firewall backend vpp` | `applyTables` in `firewall/plugins/irr/irr.go` builds a table of its own with named sets (`buildIfaceTables`); the VPP verifier refuses named sets in `ApplyAll`. A firewall term naming an IRR AS-SET or a domain group is not this case: it is a `MatchInSet` in the firewall's own table, which the VPP verifier already refuses at commit | The commit succeeds, then the table is refused at apply, as in H |
| I | `connection-state`, `flow-offload`, `mark-set` under `firewall backend vpp` | YANG `ze:backend "nft"` on the three nodes in the firewall module; `Verify` accepts `MatchConnState`, `FlowOffload`, `SetMark` | The commit refuses them. The verifier support is unreachable from operator config. Two declarations of one fact disagree. |

**Behavior to preserve:**
- Every existing `ze:backend` refusal, its path and its wording, except that the list of supporting backends it prints names `kernel` where it named `netlink`, `nft` or `tc` (N-1). The `.ci` tests `test/parse/iface-vpp-rejects-bridge.ci`, `test/parse/iface-vpp-rejects-tunnel.ci` and `test/parse/iface-vpp-rejects-veth.ci` stay green unchanged: each holds `interface backend vpp` and nothing a new refusal reaches. `test/parse/pppoe-vpp-backend-refused.ci` holds nothing a new refusal reaches either, but its expectation `supported: netlink` becomes `supported: kernel` with the PPPoE provider key (row U-14), judged by `ze-test-judge`.
- What `test/traffic/traffic-vpp-reject-hfsc.ci` and `test/parse/iface-vpp-accepts-wireguard.ci` prove, with their inputs converted (Repository users the refusals reach, rows U-1 and U-5). Neither stays unchanged. The hfsc candidate selects `traffic control backend vpp` with no `interface` block, so `interface backend` is its default (`netlink` today, `kernel` after N-1): that is case D, which AC-4 refuses in `LoadConfig` before the reload transaction reaches the traffic verifier, so its `qdisc hfsc: not supported by backend vpp` expectation would go red. The wireguard file is case B, which AC-2 refuses, because `vpp plugins wireguard` defaults to `false` (`internal/component/vpp/yang/ze-vpp-conf.yang`).
- Every refusal of the VPP firewall and traffic verifiers, at verify and in `ApplyAll`. A firewall term naming an IRR AS-SET or a domain group under `firewall backend vpp` stays refused at commit by the `MatchInSet` case.
- `registry.DataPlaneWriter` and the `doctor-plugin-no-dataplane-writer` row.
- The PPPoE session provider gate and its `.ci`.
- A configuration with no VPP selection anywhere behaves exactly as today.
- A build without `ze_vpp` still refuses `backend vpp` through `ConfigNeeds` and names the tag.

**Behavior to change:**
- A, B, C, D, E, H, J and K become refusals at commit, start, reload and `ze config validate` (AC-1 to AC-5, AC-8): A, C, D, E and J through the data-plane verdict in the config doors; B through the VPP interface backend's `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` tree validator; H and K through the firewall's `firewall-producer-tables` tree validator. `LoadConfig` runs both validators before any plugin starts. E includes flow export conntrack flow records (C-4 (a), AC-15), and IPsec is refused at daemon start like every other mismatch (C-5, AC-16).
- F becomes a refusal at commit and `ze config validate` (AC-6), from the firewall VPP verifier. At daemon start it keeps today's route through the component apply: the verifier `ApplyAll` runs refuses it.
- G stops depending on whether a VPP connector exists: the static plugin's named-table backend follows the configured data plane (AC-7).
- A `fib kernel` block beside a `fib vpp` block under `interface backend vpp` stays accepted (C-3 (a), AC-14). A `fib kernel` block with no `fib vpp` block beside it is refused, for now, with the fix that adds `fib vpp` (P-1 (b), "atm"): today it commits and VPP's FIB gets no route. `fibPluginChosen` is unchanged, so an explicit `fib` block still always wins.
- `sysctl` stays accepted under `interface backend vpp`, all of it, as the second host-side use (Q-1 (b)). It is applied to the Linux kernel exactly as on any data plane, so a setting about kernel behaviour works unchanged on a VPP box. Only a key about packet forwarding, for example `rp_filter` or `forwarding`, acts on the packets that reach Linux, those VPP punts through the LCP TAP, rather than on the packets VPP forwards (`plan/journal/silent-fall-through.md`, the `rpf-check` row). That is documented, not refused (Known Limitations, the generated page).
- Every door judges with the `--plugin` arguments its process was started with, read from one record the `ze` dispatcher writes, so a plugin named on the command line is judged by the same verdict at validate, at start, at reload and at every commit door (Q-2, AC-3, AC-18). Today only the start and the reload hold the list: `ze config validate`, `ze doctor`, the pushed-candidate judge, the managed push handler and every commit door judge without it, and the ephemeral daemon `ze config edit` starts when none runs is started without it. That daemon is started with the recorded list as global `--plugin` arguments before `start`, so its own dispatcher records the same list (AC-18).
- Doctor: A, C, D, E, J and a `fib kernel` block with no `fib vpp` block under `vpp` are reported under `doctor-dataplane-mismatch` (AC-9); B stays under `doctor-vpp-wireguard`, from the same function as its tree validator; F, H and K keep the verifier's words.
- I: the three `ze:backend "nft"` annotations are deleted and the VPP verifier decides (C-2 (b), AC-17). `connection-state` and `mark-set` become accepted on VPP within the verifier's limits; `flow-offload` stays refused, by the `ze:backend` annotation of the `flowtable` list it must name (`"nft"` today, `"kernel"` after N-1), not by the verifier.
- N-1: the three selectors take `kernel` and `vpp`, each defaults to `kernel`, and an old value (`netlink`, `nft`, `tc`) is refused at every door with the fix that names `kernel` (AC-19). Every `ze:backend` annotation, backend registration name, `Registration.DataPlane` value and plane-keyed registry key names `kernel` where it named `netlink`, `nft` or `tc`, and every repository user is converted in the same work (Repository users the refusals reach, rows U-14 to U-21).
- The four per-component gate calls become one walk owned by the config component.
- `docs/guide/dataplane-features.md` becomes generated.

## Existing Mechanisms (MANDATORY)

| Mechanism | Where (file + symbol, or page + section) | What it does today | This spec (extends, replaces, or does not apply, with the reason) |
|-----------|------------------------------------------|--------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `ze:backend` annotation and walker | `internal/component/config/backend_gate.go` `validateBackendFeatures`; `docs/architecture/core-design.md` section 14a | Refuses a node the selected backend does not implement, for one root and one backend at a time | Extends: the walker is kept. The four per-component call sites are replaced by one walk over the whole config tree in the config doors, so an annotation in any module is judged |
| Backend verifiers | `firewall.RegisterVerifier`, `traffic.RegisterVerifier` | Refuse semantic limits an annotation cannot express | Extends: copp and firewall-irr register config table builders whose tables the firewall's `firewall-producer-tables` tree validator runs through the candidate backend's registered verifier (H, K); the NAT source match becomes a refusal (F). Does not apply to B: `iface` gains no verifier registry, because a verifier run from the interface component's `InProcessConfigVerifier` is handed the `interface` and `class-of-service` roots only, never `vpp plugins wireguard`, and a commit that changes only the `vpp` root would not run it |
| `registry.ConfigNeed` | `internal/component/plugin/registry/config_need.go`; `internal/component/config/optional_provider.go` | "A leaf holding this value needs this plugin in the build", evaluated over the parsed tree in `LoadConfig` and `VerifyPluginConfigMapTransition` | Extends by shape: the new declaration is evaluated at the same two doors by the same component: `LoadConfig`, and `verifyTree`, the caller of `VerifyPluginConfigMapTransition` that holds the tree the verdict and the walk read. Not reused as is: it answers build presence, never data-plane agreement |
| Original-tree validators | `internal/component/config/tree_validators.go` `RegisterTreeValidator`; the PPPoE component registers `pppoe-backend` (`validateBackendGate`) | A registered whole-tree check run by `ValidateCustomSections` at boot, reload, `ze config validate` and the SSH editor commit; the PPPoE gate reads `interface backend` with its schema default | Extends by shape: the PPPoE gate is the precedent for reading the selector from the schema default. Not the home of the verdict: its loop stops at the first refusal, and one tree validator per feature would be the hand check per plugin set aside below. Extends for AC-8: the firewall component registers one, `firewall-producer-tables`, for every producer whose tables the configuration alone fixes; producers register builders into it, so no producer adds a validator of its own, and it joins every refusal it finds. Extends for AC-2: the VPP interface backend registers `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` from its own `init()`. It is a limit of one backend that spans two roots (`interface` and `vpp`), not a plane mismatch, so it is neither a verdict declaration nor a hand check of plane agreement. `validateOriginalTree` hands every validator the whole candidate tree at every door, whichever root the commit changed, so a commit that only turns `vpp plugins wireguard` off is judged. L2TP uses the same `init()` route to reach the config component with no registration, which is how L2TP declares its plane |
| Start keeps a configuration a commit would refuse | `docs/architecture/ike/ipsec-8-ikev2-child-xfrm.md`, "ICMP error messages carried by a Child SA": owner decision of 2026-11-19; `warnICMPOverlapAtStart` | For the IPsec block alone: start installs what it can, logs each problem at Error, and `ze doctor` reports it | Does not apply to a data-plane mismatch: Thomas set this start mode aside for it (C-5), so `vpn ipsec` under `interface backend vpp` is refused at start like every other mismatch. The IPsec rule stays for the block's own conflicts. No start mode, mark or commit lock is built |
| `ProgramsFIB`, `DataPlane`, `NeedsDataPlane` | `internal/component/plugin/registry/registry.go`; `internal/component/plugin/registry/dataplane_writer.go` `DataPlaneWriter` | A FIB plugin declares the data plane it reaches; boot, reload and doctor share one judgment | Extends: the same vocabulary, `kernel` and `vpp` after N-1 (`netlink` and `vpp` today), names the plane of every backend and feature. `DataPlane` on a FIB plugin feeds the new verdict (A): a FIB plugin is in use when the operator wrote its config root, and the writer `appendDataPlaneWriter` adds is the selected plane's by construction. `fib-kernel` declares `DataPlane: "kernel"` the same way (`"netlink"` today), and sets one mark on its own registration, the host-side mark: this declaration acts on the Linux host itself, beside the selected data plane. The mark says nothing about what the declaration does there; each setter's own use is the one the decision row names: `fib-kernel` programs the Linux route table for the router's own traffic (C-3 (a)). `sysctl` declares the kernel plane and sets the same mark (Q-1 (b)): it sets the Linux kernel's own tunables. The verdict reads the mark and reports no plane mismatch for either under `interface backend vpp` (C-3 (a), AC-5, AC-14). One rule over these same fields keeps a marked FIB writer from standing alone: when a configured `ProgramsFIB` plugin carries the mark and declares a plane other than the selected one, and no configured `ProgramsFIB` plugin declares the selected plane, the verdict refuses, with the fix naming the config root of the registration that declares `ProgramsFIB` and the selected plane (P-1 (b), AC-14). `fibPluginChosen` and `appendDataPlaneWriter` are unchanged, so an explicit `fib` block still always wins. A FIB plugin that declares no `DataPlane` (`fib-p4`) declares nothing to compare and is not judged |
| `kernelcap.Capability` | `internal/component/kernelcap/kernelcap.go`; `plan/release/validation/spec-kernel-capability-gate.md` | The owning package declares a host requirement and an `InUse(tree)` predicate; `Refuse` is called at start, reload and validate, and `MustRegister` adds the doctor row | Extends by shape: the data-plane declaration carries the same in-use predicate. Not reused as is: its probe asks the host kernel, and this verdict needs no probe |
| Plugin selection of a candidate | `internal/component/config/loader.go` `ExtractPluginsFromTree`, `MergeCliPlugins`, `finalizeConfigLoad` | Reads the `plugin internal` and `plugin external` blocks into the selected plugins, each carrying the registered plugin its `use` leaf names, and merges the plugins named on the command line | Extends: a declaring plugin that owns no config root (flowspec-firewall) declares no tree predicate. The verdict asks this selection whether the candidate selects the plugin, matched on the registered name the `use` leaf holds, never on the block key. No plugin spells the `plugin` block's path. One function of the config component decides a candidate's selection for the verdict: `ExtractPluginsFromTree`, merged through `MergeCliPlugins` with the command-line plugins of the process (the record of the next row) and with the command-line plugins its caller holds, as `finalizeConfigLoad` merges before `ExpandDependencies`. `loadConfig` hands it its own `cliPlugins` parameter; `verifyTree` holds no list and hands it none. `MergeCliPlugins` keeps one entry per resolved name, so in a daemon, where the parameter and the record are the same slice, the union is that slice. `verifyTree` and `loadConfig` both call it, so the verdict sees the same selection at every door (Q-2). A compiled-in plugin's declaration comes from the registry; an external program named there is asked for its declaration as `spec-explicit-plugin-and-attach-config` defines (query mode), which that spec owns |
| Process-wide record of what the process started with | `internal/component/config/declared_schemas.go` `declaredStep`, `RecordStartedPlugins` (written once by `runYANGConfig`), `RecordDaemonUnanswered`; `ai/doors/config-yang.md`, "Who depends on it from outside", the external plugin author row ("inside a running daemon the step answers from the set boot published") | One mutex-guarded record in the config component. The process writes it once, before it judges or serves anything, and every door of that process reads it through `SchemaWithDeclaredPlugins` with no parameter threaded to the door: the SSH and web editors, the API and gNMI hooks, the uploads and `ze config validate` all get the daemon's external-plugin set this way today | Extends: the record gains one value, the plugins named on the command line of this process, and one writer, `RecordCommandLinePlugins`. The `ze` dispatcher calls it once, where `--plugin` is parsed and before any verb runs, so the daemon, `ze config validate`, `ze doctor` and the offline editing sessions each judge with the list their own process was started with. The selection function of the row above is the one reader that decides the verdict's selection. Two other readers only display or forward the list and decide nothing: the SSH upload receipt names it in its fix line (`cmd/ze/hub/config_upload.go`), and `startEphemeralDaemon` (`cmd_edit.go`) passes it as global `--plugin` arguments to the daemon it starts, whose own dispatcher records it again. This replaces the threading the earlier draft planned (a plugin list handed to `cmdValidate`, `ValidateContent`, `NewConfigValidator`, `configValidationHook` and the upload runtime): see Alternatives set aside |
| Session provider registry | `internal/component/l2tp/pppoe/backend_gate.go` `validateBackendGate` | Refuses PPPoE access when no provider is registered for the interface backend | Does not apply as a target: it is already a registration keyed by the data plane name and already refuses with a fix. It stays, and its kernel provider's key becomes `kernel` with the plane (N-1, U-14) |
| Backend registration names and plane-keyed registries | `iface.RegisterBackend`, `firewall.RegisterBackend`, `traffic.RegisterBackend` and the verifier registries keyed by the same name; `internal/component/l2tp/pppoe/dataplane` (session providers), `internal/core/mplsfib` `RegisterForwardingReader` (MPLS readers), `Registration.DataPlane` | Each registers under a name the selector leaf holds; the kernel backends register as `netlink`, `nft` and `tc`, the PPPoE kernel provider and the MPLS kernel reader as `netlink`, `fib-kernel` declares `netlink` | Extends (N-1): every name becomes the plane name, `kernel` or `vpp`, so a backend's registered name is the plane it programs and no second plane argument is added: one fact, declared once. A selector value that names no registered backend is refused by the verdict with the fix that sets the selector's schema default (AC-19). No alias is registered for an old name |
| Hand checks in plugins | `internal/plugins/vrrp/groups.go` `validateGroups`; `internal/plugins/container/network/backend.go` `checkBackends` | Repeat a YANG annotation the interface gate already judges (vrrp); hardcode both schema defaults and run the gate on a foreign root (container) | Replaces: the VRRP backend branch and the container gate call are deleted once the engine walk judges their annotations, and `interfaceBackendDefault` goes with the call, its only reader. The container masquerade and port rules stay, as they are semantic, and still need the firewall backend: `checkBackends` reads it through the config component's selector resolution, the function the verdict uses (the `backend` leaf the firewall registration declares as its selector, as written, or else that leaf's schema default), so `firewallBackendDefault` is deleted and no Go literal of a default remains, as the PPPoE gate already reads `interface backend` from its schema default |
| Doctor checks | `vpp-wireguard-plugin` (code `doctor-vpp-wireguard`) in `internal/plugins/iface/vpp/doctor.go`; `doctor-plugin-no-dataplane-writer` in `internal/component/plugin/doctor/register_doctor_codes.go`; `doctor-vrrp-backend-unusable` in `internal/plugins/vrrp/doctor.go` | Report a problem the commit accepts (wireguard), or repeat a refusal (writer, vrrp) | Extends: the wireguard check calls the function the new `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` tree validator calls; one new code reports the data-plane verdict; the VRRP backend code goes with its Go branch, its row reported by the verdict's code |
| Firewall table registry | `internal/component/firewall/registry.go` `RegisterTables`, `ApplyAll` | Merges every producer's tables, autoloads the OS default backend when none is loaded, verifies and applies | Extends: a producer whose tables the configuration alone fixes (copp, the IRR interface bindings) registers a config table builder, and the `firewall-producer-tables` tree validator runs the same registered verifier over those tables before any plugin starts; the run-time producers stay judged in `ApplyAll` |
| Implied configuration display | `ai/META-DECISIONS.md`, "CLI, config, and daemon surfaces"; `plan/release/fixes/spec-bus-2-implied-display-and-grant-refusals.md` (its title read, not its body) | Shows what Ze does on the operator's behalf, such as the plugin that serves a block the operator wrote. The engine already loads the selected plane's FIB writer when a route producer runs and no `fib` block is written (`appendDataPlaneWriter`) | Does not apply. Thomas refused `fib kernel` alone under `interface backend vpp` (P-1 (b)), so Ze adds no FIB writer the operator did not write and there is nothing new to display; C-1 set aside the host-side scope it would have displayed |
| YANG `must` | YANG modules | Not used for backend agreement | Does not apply: a `must` in one module would name another module's path, which puts a feature name in a schema that does not own it |
| Generated reference page | `internal/le/doc/yangcontract/drift.go` `checkPipeOperatorReference`, `writeGenerated`; `docs/features/pipe-operators.generated.md` | A page written from declarations and compared by a doc check | Extends: the same route generates the data-plane page |
| Retired-command sweep | `internal/le/doc/check/retired.go` `retiredRecord`, `retiredRecords`, `retiredExcluded`, `retiredOwnedRowsAfter`, `retiredExceptions`, `sweepRetired`, `runRetiredCheck`; `internal/le/doc/check/repository.go` `walkTracked`; `docs/architecture/core-design.md`, "Command engine and composition roots", the `le doc check retired-commands` paragraph | `le doc check retired-commands` walks every tracked file through `walkTracked`, the one tracked-file walk it shares with the links sweep, and skips a file that holds a NUL byte. `retiredExcluded` skips the vendored tree, `retiredDeclarations` and the historical records `retiredRecords` declares, each a `retiredRecord` of a path and a reason: a file, or a directory when the path ends in `/`, matched by path alone, four reboot snapshots among them by path under `retiredRebootSnapshotWhy`. Inside a file it skips the owned journal rows section of a spec under `plan/` (`retiredOwnedRowsAfter`) and the reseal notes of the sealed package. It names the file and line of every other line that still names a retired form, unless a line exception covers it (`retiredExceptions`: the file, the old spelling, the text of each covered line, the reason). `runRetiredCheck` exits 1 on a finding and 2 when a tracked file cannot be read | Extends (F-1 of AC-20). `retiredRecord` gains one field, the snapshot manifest whose pinned files the record covers. The record matching moves out of `retiredExcluded` into one function that answers, for a checkout root, whether a tracked path is covered by a record; it reads the manifest once, before the walk, and an absent manifest pins nothing. The four snapshot path records are deleted and one record naming `plan/post-release/engineering/tooling-one-off-reboot-assets/manifest.json` under `retiredRebootSnapshotWhy` replaces them (`ai/rules/no-layering.md`). A manifest that cannot be read or parsed, an entry with a set digest and no path, or a pinned path outside the manifest's directory makes the sweep answer exit 2, naming the manifest as unreadable, never a record that covers nothing. `retiredExcluded` keeps the vendored tree and `retiredDeclarations`. The retired selector value sweep is a new action beside it that reuses, and never copies: `walkTracked`, with the same NUL-byte skip; the records, through the same function (F-1); the owned journal rows skip, through `retiredOwnedRowsAfter`; and the line-exception shape of `retiredExceptions` for its own line exceptions. It does not reuse the rename map, the reseal-note skip or the citation judgement, which judge le commands, not selector values |

## Design documents

One row or more per page, and one row per decision, that
`./le spec design documents spec plan/release/fixes/spec-dataplane-compatibility-guard.md`
derives. Each verdict was read against the section the design touches; a
`silent` row names what the section is about instead.

| Document | Section | Derived from | Binding statement | Verdict | How this design stands against it |
|----------|---------|--------------|-------------------|---------|-----------------------------------|
| `ai/doors/system.md` | One schema, one command registry, every interface | system door | "A backend that cannot apply a config exactly refuses it at verify." | agrees | F, H and K move a late or silent outcome to a refusal at commit, and H and K to one at start too; G replaces a silent kernel fallback with the configured plane or an error |
| `ai/doors/forwarding.md` | Boundaries | door of the files modified | "Never add a list of FIB plugin names anywhere." | agrees | The verdict reads declarations from the registry and names no plugin |
| `ai/doors/forwarding.md` | Boundaries | door of the files modified | "The firewall table registry is process-local." | agrees | The config table builders are process-local too: copp and firewall-irr register theirs from `init()`, as they call `RegisterTables` in process at apply, and the `firewall-producer-tables` validator runs them in the process that loads or validates the configuration |
| `ai/doors/config-yang.md` | Traps | door of `internal/component/config` | "`ze config validate` never calls a live `OnConfigVerify`." | agrees | The verdict runs in `LoadConfig`, beside `refuseAbsentOptionalProviders`, and in `verifyTree` (`plugin_verify.go`), beside `refuseUnresolvableDependencies`, which `ze config validate` reaches through `VerifyPluginConfig` with no daemon |
| `ai/doors/plugin-infra.md` | Boundaries | door of `internal/component/plugin` | "The engine never enumerates plugins." | agrees | Each backend and feature declares its plane from its own `init()` |
| `ai/doors/system-plugins.md` | Boundaries | door of `internal/plugins` | "A plugin owns its whole surface." | agrees | Each plugin's plane, predicate and verifier live in its folder |
| `ai/doors/access-security.md` | Where it sits | door of `internal/component/ike`, `internal/component/l2tp` | "Two IKE packages stay ungated on purpose." | agrees | The IPsec plane is declared by the IKE engine registration, and OSPF's use of the XFRM seam is not judged as IPsec in use |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | 14a. Commit-Time Backend Capability Gate | `// Design:` header of `internal/component/config/backend_gate.go`; page this spec modifies | "Absent annotation = unrestricted." | agrees | The annotation keeps its meaning; a feature with no annotation is judged by its declared plane, a separate fact |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | 14a. Commit-Time Backend Capability Gate | page this spec modifies | "iface, firewall, and traffic call the gate from `OnConfigure` (startup) and from the parse their `OnConfigVerify` (reload) and their `InProcessConfigVerifier` share." | departs | The config component runs one walk at its two doors; the three components and the container plugin stop calling it |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "A commit fails when it configures a feature that programs only the Linux kernel, or that reads only what the Linux kernel sees of the traffic, while the interfaces run on VPP. Two host-side uses stay accepted, so that mixed setups remain possible once VRFs are added: a `fib kernel` block beside a `fib vpp` block, which programs the Linux route table for the router's own traffic, and `sysctl`, which sets the Linux kernel's own tunables. A `fib kernel` block with no `fib vpp` block beside it is refused, for now." | agrees | AC-3 and AC-5 implement the refusal. AC-15 refuses the conntrack flow records, the feature that only reads (C-4 (a)). The host-side mark is set by two registrations only, `fib-kernel` and `sysctl`: AC-14 keeps `fib kernel` accepted beside `fib vpp` (C-3 (a)) and refuses it alone, by the rule that a marked FIB writer of another plane needs the selected plane's writer configured beside it (P-1 (b)); AC-5 keeps all of `sysctl` accepted (Q-1 (b)). GTSM and every other kernel-only feature stay refused. The row was amended on 2026-11-23 with Thomas's answers to C-3, C-4, Q-1 and P-1; it was not yet committed |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "A configuration that holds such a mismatch is refused at daemon start as well as at commit, reload and `ze config validate`, by one verdict that is the same at each." | agrees | The verdict is called in `LoadConfig`, which start and every reload call with the same arguments, and in `verifyTree`, which validate and the commit doors call. No input tells the loader that a load is a start, and no declaration carries a start mark (AC-16). The plugin selection it judges is the same at every door, because no door supplies it: one function answers the candidate's selection from the tree and from the command-line plugins the process recorded where `--plugin` is parsed, and in `LoadConfig` also from the same list the loader is handed (Q-2, AC-3, AC-18, R-9). Inside a daemon that is the list the daemon runs with, at start, at reload and at every commit door (SSH and web editor, REST and gRPC, gNMI Set, the uploads, the managed push handler); in `ze config validate` it is the list that command was given; the ephemeral daemon `ze config edit` starts when none runs is started with that command's list as `--plugin` arguments, so it judges with it too. A plugin named only on the command line therefore meets the same verdict everywhere |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "The VPP firewall verifier, not a YANG annotation, decides whether `connection-state` and `mark-set` are accepted on VPP. The `ze:backend` annotation of `flow-offload` is deleted with theirs; `flow-offload` stays refused on VPP because VPP has no flowtable and the `flowtable` list keeps its `ze:backend "nft"` annotation." | departs | N-1 renames the `flowtable` list's annotation to `ze:backend "kernel"`, so the row's words "keeps its `ze:backend "nft"` annotation" become "keeps its `ze:backend "kernel"` annotation". The decision stands; only the value it names changes, and the row is corrected in the same work once Thomas approves the departure (Documents departed from). AC-17 deletes the three annotations. The verifier then accepts `connection-state` for established and related and accepts `mark-set` within its classify limits. `flow-offload` stays refused, and the refusal does not come from the verifier: `validateAction` (`internal/component/firewall/validate.go`) makes every `flow-offload` name a flowtable its table declares, and the `flowtable` list keeps `ze:backend "nft"` (`ze-firewall-conf.yang`), so the engine walk refuses the flowtable node at every door, `LoadConfig` included. No annotation is left on `connection-state` or `mark-set`, and each owes its real-VPP proof before a page announces it. `flow-offload`'s own annotation is deleted, as Thomas confirmed (P-2: "p2 delete") |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | 14. Interface Management | `// Design:` page of `internal/component/iface/backend.go`; page this spec modifies | "YANG `backend` leaf (default: `netlink`). `RegisterBackend`/`LoadBackend` in `backend.go`" | departs | The leaf stays the one data-plane selector and its default is read from the schema, but the default becomes `kernel` and `netlink` is refused (N-1, AC-19). `RegisterBackend` keeps its signature: the name a backend registers under is the plane it programs (AC-11). The row is rewritten (Documents departed from) |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Every data-plane selector names the plane it programs, `kernel` or `vpp`: `interface backend`, `firewall backend` and `traffic control backend` take `kernel` where they took `netlink`, `nft` and `tc`, and each defaults to `kernel`; the `fib` blocks already name `kernel` and `vpp`. The plane a registration declares and the value a `ze:backend` annotation names are the same word. A second implementation on the same plane, such as eBPF, takes a value of its own when it exists. In Thomas's words (voice-transcribed): "should tc and netfilter be renamed to kernel? so we have vpp and kernel?", then "please do it"." | agrees | AC-19 renames the three selectors' values and defaults, the `ze:backend` annotations, the backend registration names, `fib-kernel`'s `DataPlane`, and the PPPoE provider and MPLS reader keys, and converts every repository user (rows U-14 to U-21). No alias is registered and no conversion is written: an old value meets the verdict's refusal of a selector value no backend answers, whose fix sets the schema default, `kernel`. AC-11 adds no plane argument, because the registered name is the plane. The eBPF cost is accepted: no second kernel implementation exists, and none is designed here |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "BGP egress next-hop rewriting belongs exclusively in the export filter chain, not under session configuration (`session { next-hop }`). Ze automatically appends `next-hop auto` at the end of the export chain when no earlier export filter has already set a next-hop outcome (`self`, `unchanged`, or an explicit address), so ordinary eBGP rewrites to self while iBGP and route-server clients preserve the received next hop. In Thomas's words (voice-transcribed): "I would think that all the rewrite should be in the prefix export and not under configuration. I believe we should automatically add to the export prefix list the auto at the end of the list if not defined with something else. So if no one, for example, used a self or unchanged, we should automatically change it on eBGP. Because otherwise we have two ways to modify packets on the way out to the peer.", then "Unify into the export chain it is the way I want it."" | agrees | This spec touches no BGP export chain, next-hop rewrite or `session` leaf. Its only BGP inputs are read-only: the GTSM in-use predicate reads a peer's or group's `ttl max`, `ttl set` and `ttl min` leaves (AC-5), and the edit to `internal/component/bgp/reactor/link_scope_neighbors.go` changes one hint's backend name (N-1). Neither adds a second egress rewrite surface or a session-level next-hop knob |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Egress export chains configure next-hop rewriting through one underlying `policychain.Sets` next-hop intent exposed in two authoring forms: `policy { modify <name> { set { next-hop self \| unchanged \| auto \| <ip>; } } }` for matched/conditional rules, and built-in chain references `next-hop:self`, `next-hop:unchanged`, and `next-hop:auto` for peer-wide policy without a wrapper `modify` block. Selected by Thomas on 2026-11-23 ("Option A: Extend policy modify (self\|unchanged\|auto\|<ip>) AND provide built-in next-hop:self / next-hop:unchanged / next-hop:auto chain refs")." | agrees | This spec adds no policy, `modify` block or chain reference, so the one next-hop intent and its two authoring forms stand as the row states |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Built-in `next-hop:auto`, `next-hop:self`, and `next-hop:unchanged` are BGP engine steps compiled into `policychain.Chain` (parallel to `loop-detection`), never external plugin RPCs or a `pkg/plugin/rpc.FilterInsertion` tail extension. Config loading appends `next-hop:auto` at the tail of every export chain unless an unconditional `next-hop:self`, `next-hop:unchanged`, or `next-hop:auto` is already present, leaving `Policy.Configured()` false for unconfigured chains; operators override via `next-hop:unchanged`, and `inactive:next-hop:auto` is refused. Selected by Thomas on 2026-11-23 ("Option A: Built-in BGP engine step appended at config compile time (like loop-detection; zero RPC; override via next-hop:unchanged, no inactive:next-hop:auto)")." | agrees | This spec changes no chain step, no `pkg/plugin/rpc.FilterInsertion` and no config-load append, so the built-in steps stay engine steps as the row states |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Within an export chain, explicit next-hop sets (`self`, `unchanged`, `auto`, or an IP address) use standard last-write-wins folding while the auto-appended trailing `next-hop:auto` acts only when next-hop is still unset. For a whole-UPDATE raw override (`wireOverride`), Ze compares each emitted next-hop field against the received UPDATE (matching `forward_med.go` provenance): trailing `auto` is skipped only when the raw override changed the next-hop bytes or an earlier chain member already selected `unchanged`/`self`/explicit. Selected by Thomas on 2026-11-23 ("Option A: Provenance check on raw overrides (like MED): skip trailing auto only when the raw override changed the next-hop bytes or an earlier member set unchanged/self")." | agrees | This spec changes no next-hop fold and no raw-override provenance check |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Explicit next-hop addresses in `policy { modify <name> { set { next-hop <ip>; } } }` and `policychain.Sets.NextHop` support both IPv4 (attr 3) and IPv6/MP_REACH (through `NextHopProfile` and `ValidatePeerGlobalNextHop`) on both export and import chains. Egress-only next-hop modes (`self`, `unchanged`, `auto`), whether written as built-in `next-hop:*` refs or inside `policy { modify }`, are refused on import chains at config load. Selected by Thomas on 2026-11-23 ("Option A: Support IPv4 + IPv6/MP_REACH for explicit next-hop on both export and import; refuse self/unchanged/auto on import at config load")." | agrees | This spec changes no next-hop address handling on either chain and refuses nothing on an import chain |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Auto-appended trailing `next-hop:auto` applies only to forwarded routes and preserves the originator-chosen next hop on locally originated routes (static config routes, ExaBGP `announce`, API announcements). Explicit export-chain next-hop actions (`next-hop:self`, `modify { set { next-hop ... } }`) apply uniformly to both forwarded and locally originated routes. Selected by Thomas on 2026-11-23 ("Option A: Trailing next-hop:auto applies to forwarded routes only (preserves static/ExaBGP/API explicit next-hop); explicit next-hop:self / modify next-hop applies to both forwarded and originated routes")." | agrees | This spec changes no chain member and no rule for which routes a member acts on, so locally originated routes keep the next hop their originator chose, as the row states |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Every automatic filter-chain member Ze inserts — both built-in BGP engine steps (`next-hop:auto` at the export tail, `loop-detection` at the import head) and plugin-declared `FilterInsertions` — is published through one queryable implied-filter seam carrying direction, position, reference and `implied-by` reason so `\| implied` displays every extra chain member alongside the operator's written filter list. In Thomas's words (2026-11-23): "Can you make sure that \| implied show the extra chain members when it displays the filter ?"." | agrees | This spec inserts no filter-chain member, so the implied-filter seam and `\| implied` have nothing new to publish |
| `docs/architecture/testing/verify-freshness-scope.md` | Native stage execution | `// Design:` page of `internal/le/verify/engine/stages.go` | - | silent | How stages run, record and certify; AC-20 adds one `doc check` stage beside `retired-commands` and changes no execution, selection or certificate rule |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | Command engine and composition roots | `// Design:` page of `internal/le/doc/check/retired.go`; page this spec modifies | "`le doc check retired-commands` reads the map and names the file and line of every tracked line that still names an old form, outside the declared historical records and exceptions." | agrees | The manifest record is a declared historical record: it is declared once in `retiredRecords`, beside the file and directory records, and covers exactly the snapshots the manifest pins, each an inert source snapshot identified by its original commit and digest. The retired-command sweep therefore still names every tracked line outside the declared records and exceptions, and the README, the manifest itself and a file under `base/` or `unfinished/` the manifest does not pin stay swept. An unreadable manifest makes the sweep answer 2, as an unreadable tracked file already does, so no record silently covers nothing. The paragraph keeps every statement it makes about the retired-command sweep and gains one sentence on the retired selector value sweep (Files to Modify) |
| `docs/architecture/site-facts.md` | Published Facts Come From Committed Data | anchored to `internal/le/verify/engine/stages.go` | - | silent | The site facts stage; the added `doc check` stage publishes no fact |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | 14a. Commit-Time Backend Capability Gate | page this spec modifies | "An empty active backend means the config names none and this OS has no default (netlink, nft and tc are the Linux defaults)." | departs | After N-1 `kernel` is the Linux default of all three selectors; the sentence is rewritten (Documents departed from) |
| `docs/architecture/core-design.md` | 14a. Commit-Time Backend Capability Gate | page this spec modifies | "iface: `bridge`, `tunnel`, `wireguard`, `veth`, `mirror` annotated `ze:backend "netlink"`." | departs | Every `ze:backend` annotation names `kernel` where it named `netlink`, `nft` or `tc` (N-1); the "Initial coverage" row is rewritten with the AC-17 deletions (Documents departed from) |

Firewall and Traffic Control | `// Design:` page of `internal/component/firewall/backend.go` and `internal/component/traffic/backend.go` | "Two independent components, each with its own backend interface and data model." | agrees | Neither component imports the other or iface; the config component compares their selections through registry declarations only |

| `ai/doors/forwarding.md` | Boundaries | door of the files modified; page this spec modifies | "Feature support is declared with `ze:backend` in YANG and checked by `config.validateBackendFeatures` from `OnConfigure` and `OnConfigVerify`" | departs | The walk moves to the config doors and no component calls it (AC-10); Files to Modify already rewrites this bullet |

| `docs/features/interfaces.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "`ze init` claims every discovered device by default, including the ones another program owns. `ze init --confirm` asks about each discovered device and writes only the ones the operator confirms" | agrees | This spec changes no `ze init` behavior and no device discovery |
| `docs/features/interfaces.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "`ze init --confirm` takes only an explicit answer: `yes`, `y`, `no` or `n`. Anything else, an empty line included, is not accepted, and the same device is asked again" | agrees | This spec adds no prompt and changes no `ze init` answer |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Ze refuses to run VPP unmanaged. When VPP is external, the operator accepts that Ze may terminate and restart it" | agrees | The verdict judges configuration only and adds no VPP lifecycle mode |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Ze knows how to manage and restart an external VPP: through a systemd unit, or by starting the binary directly, as it does with the embedded runtime" | agrees | No restart method is added, removed or selected by this spec |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "After a failure, when the VPP process is alive, forwarding continued, and Ze reconnects and can read the state it configured, Ze continues from where it is" | agrees | The verdict runs on a candidate configuration, never on a VPP failure, so it cannot tear down state recovery keeps |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "When VPP could have crashed or stopped forwarding, Ze reflects it in its own model and makes sure IGP and BGP peers are updated" | agrees | Nothing here changes how a VPP loss reaches Ze's model or its peers |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "One timer: the alive test and the loss declaration use the same time, and a probe missed inside it is not a loss. This narrows the row "After a failure, when the VPP process is alive": Ze continues from where it is only through form (1) below, inside the timer, and a declared loss is never adopted" | agrees | No timer or loss declaration is touched |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "As soon as a loss is declared, Ze makes sure the process is terminal (kill through the retained pidfd, then wait for the exit proof), then restarts and reconfigures VPP. Ze is the only restarter: a systemd unit it drives does not restart VPP itself (Restart=no)" | agrees | No restart path is touched |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | ""Recovered" has exactly two forms. (1) Inside the single timer Ze reconnects to the same pidfd-proven process and a full readback of what it installed (interfaces, LCP pairs, owned routes, PPPoE sessions) matches: Ze continues, with no kill and no withdrawal. Any mismatch means form (1) failed. (2) A forced restart (kill through the retained pidfd, exit proof) followed by a correct reconfiguration, exactly as at boot. Routes are withdrawn only when neither form succeeds within the recovery deadline and the crash-loop limit" | agrees | Form (2) reconfigures from the running configuration, which the verdict already accepted, so a restart meets no new refusal |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Blackholing during the restart is accepted. Routes are withdrawn (VPP-backed interfaces down in Ze's model, so IGP and BGP react) only when neither recovery form succeeds within the recovery deadline and the crash-loop limit. This narrows the row "When VPP could have crashed or stopped forwarding": Ze updates IGP and BGP peers only then" | agrees | No withdrawal rule is touched. AC-7's static backend answers a failed VPP channel with an error and a doctor code rather than a kernel write, which adds no withdrawal |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "systemd configuration: Ze cannot be sure that an OS update will not change the unit's settings, so it detects the settings and adapts. No unit policy is required: Ze reads the unit's effective settings each time it acts on VPP, and adapts its restart to them. This supersedes the second sentence of the row "As soon as a loss is declared" (Ze the only restarter, Restart=no) and its forbids clause "or leaving the restart to systemd or another supervisor"; correcting that row needs Thomas's decision-change" | agrees | The verdict reads no unit setting and refuses no unit policy |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Two limits. The alive test and the loss declaration use the same time: a probe missed inside it is not a loss, and a failed alive test is the loss declaration. A second, separate limit then bounds the reconnect-and-verify attempt of form (1). When that limit expires without form (1), Ze makes the process terminal (kill through the retained pidfd, then wait for the exit proof), then restarts and reconfigures VPP. This supersedes the row "One timer" (its single timer, "a declared loss is never adopted" and its forbids clause "a second window after the declaration"), and narrows the row "As soon as a loss is declared": the kill follows the second limit, not the declaration" | agrees | No limit is touched |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "Form (1) adopts on the presence of data ("We should take the presence of data as a sign that the software is still running and reconciliation may be then happening in the background."). Inside the reconnect-and-verify limit, the same pidfd-proven process answering over the API with the data Ze configured is the sign it is alive: Ze adopts it, with no kill and no withdrawal, and reconciles in the background, as the fib-vpp replay and sweep and the iface reconcile do. A mismatch the background reconciliation finds is repaired, not treated as a loss. Form (2) stays a forced restart followed by a correct reconfiguration, exactly as at boot. This narrows the row "Recovered has exactly two forms": a full readback match is no longer required, and a mismatch no longer fails form (1)" | agrees | The fib-vpp and iface reconciles keep their behavior; the verdict adds no readback |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "A recovery deadline and a crash-loop limit (N restarts inside a window) bound recovery. Past either, Ze withdraws: VPP-backed interfaces go down in Ze's model, so IGP and BGP react, and ze doctor reports it. Blackholing while Ze restarts inside both limits is accepted. The default values are proposed at design review for Thomas's approval. This adopts the recovery deadline and the crash-loop limit that the rows "Recovered has exactly two forms" and "Blackholing during the restart is accepted" carried as proposals, and narrows the row "When VPP could have crashed or stopped forwarding": Ze updates IGP and BGP peers only past a limit" | agrees | No limit or withdrawal is touched; the new doctor code reports configuration only |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "After a withdrawal Ze keeps retrying recovery with a growing backoff. When VPP recovers, the VPP-backed interfaces come up in Ze's model and the routes return by themselves, and ze doctor reports the state throughout. This narrows the crash-loop limit: past it Ze withdraws and keeps restarting" | agrees | No recovery retry is touched |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "The VPP loss itself triggers no withdrawal: no VPP-backed interface goes down in Ze's model while Ze recovers. Ze's own protocol timers (BFD detection, OSPF and IS-IS dead intervals, BGP hold) keep running during recovery, as their RFCs say" | agrees | No protocol timer and no interface state is touched |
| `docs/research/vpp-deployment-reference.md` | Design decisions | decision row | "A process on VPP's API socket whose pidfd does not resolve to the configured systemd unit is reported, never refused or killed ("We should report but not refuse if the customers have changed the software, for example. It may mean that we have an out of date configuration. We should make sure the customer updates it but not cause a routing outage."). ze doctor names the process, the configured unit, that the configuration may be out of date, and the exact fix, and Ze keeps working with that process" | agrees | The verdict refuses configuration, never a VPP process |

The binding statement then holds for them too: validate and boot refuse a command-line plugin whose dependency the build lacks with the same error. The section's last sentence keeps its fact and loses its reason: these doors still do not resolve the FIB writer, because `refuseUnresolvableDependencies` adds no writer, but "the data plane is read by the `iface` component, which `config` does not import" stops holding once config reads the declared selector.
