## Data Flow (MANDATORY - see `ai/rules/architecture.md`)

### Entry Point
- A candidate configuration: at daemon start, at reload or commit, and in `ze config validate`. Format: the parsed config tree.

### Transformation Path
1. Each backend registers with its component under the name of the data plane it programs, `kernel` or `vpp` (N-1), so the registered name is the plane and no second declaration of it exists. Each component registration names its selector leaf and the config roots the selection governs.
2. Each selector-less feature registration names the data planes its forwarding state lives in and how it is in use. In use is one of two declared forms: a predicate over the candidate tree (the sampling container, a nonzero `ttl` leaf), or, for a plugin that owns no config root, "the candidate selects this plugin". L2TP and GTSM, which have no registration, declare through a registry function they call from `init()`.
3. The interface component registration marks its selector as the one that selects the data plane.
4. In `LoadConfig` and in `verifyTree` (`plugin_verify.go`, the caller of `VerifyPluginConfigMapTransition` that holds the candidate `*Tree`, beside `refuseUnresolvableDependencies`) the config component reads the declarations from the registry, reads each selector leaf (or its schema default), and resolves the selected data plane. A selector value that names no backend its component registered, and that no `ConfigNeed` names (U-4 keeps that refusal for `vpp` in a build without `ze_vpp`), is a refusal whose fix sets the selector to its schema default, read from the schema, never a literal: the old values `netlink`, `nft` and `tc` meet this refusal, and no code names them (AC-19). It reads the candidate's plugin selection from one function: `ExtractPluginsFromTree`, merged through `MergeCliPlugins` with the command-line plugins of the process and with the command-line plugins its caller holds. The process's are recorded once, by the `ze` dispatcher, where `--plugin` is parsed and before any verb runs (`RecordCommandLinePlugins`, beside `RecordStartedPlugins` in the config component's process record). The caller's are `loadConfig`'s own `cliPlugins` parameter; `verifyTree` holds none and hands none. The selection function is the one reader that decides the verdict's selection; the other readers of the record only display it (the SSH upload receipt) or forward it (`startEphemeralDaemon`), and decide nothing (Q-2). The numbered consequences:
   1. In a daemon the record holds the slice `cmdStart` hands `hub.Run`, which the start and `diskConfigLoaders` hand `LoadConfig`, so in `loadConfig` the parameter and the record are one list and the union is that list. Every commit door of that daemon reaches `verifyTree` (SSH editor, web editor commit and web upload, REST and gRPC sessions, gNMI Set, SSH upload), and the managed push handler reaches `loadConfig` with `nil`, so each judges with the plugin set the daemon runs and none is rewired (A-15). A commit is not accepted that the reload after it refuses.
   2. In `ze config validate`, `ze doctor` and the offline editing sessions the record holds the `--plugin` arguments that command was given, and nothing when it was given none.
   3. In a process the `ze` dispatcher never ran, the `le chaos run` in-process simulation (`internal/chaos/inprocess/runner.go`), the record is empty and the parameter `["ze.bgp-rs"]` is the only holder, and the verdict in `loadConfig` judges with it, because it reads the parameter (R-9).
   4. `ze config edit` with no daemon running starts an ephemeral daemon (`startEphemeralDaemon`), a second process. It passes the recorded list as global `--plugin` arguments before `start`, one `--plugin <name>` pair per entry, in the form the dispatcher parses: `<exe> --plugin <name> ... start [--web <port>] [--insecure-web] <path>`. That daemon's own dispatcher then records the same list, and its SSH editor, the door the session commits through, judges with it.
   5. `verifyTree` merges once, and `refuseUnresolvableDependencies` reads the merged selection too, so a command-line plugin whose dependency this build lacks is refused at validate as `finalizeConfigLoad` refuses it at start. A command-line name `MergeCliPlugins` cannot resolve is refused the same way at both.
   6. The function that judges takes the tree and the selection as arguments and reads no record, so it is tested with no process state; of the verdict's code, only the selection function reads the record.
5. It walks the whole config tree once with the existing walker, judging each annotated node against the selector that governs it. A node outside every declared root (the container networks) is judged against the data-plane selector.
6. It judges each in-use backend and feature: a declared plane other than the selected data plane is a mismatch. One mark exists, the host-side mark: "this declaration acts on the Linux host itself, beside the selected data plane". The mark says nothing about what the declaration does on the host. Two registrations set it, each for its own use as the decision row names it: `fib-kernel` (C-3 (a)), which programs the Linux route table for the router's own traffic, and `sysctl` (Q-1 (b)), which sets the Linux kernel's own tunables. The verdict reports no plane mismatch for a declaration that carries it, without naming its owner. One rule then reads the mark, `ProgramsFIB` and `DataPlane` over the in-use FIB plugins: a marked FIB writer of another plane with no FIB writer of the selected plane beside it is a mismatch, whose fix names the config root of the registration that declares `ProgramsFIB` and the selected plane (P-1 (b)). No declaration says how a door treats it: the verdict is the same at start, reload, commit and validate (C-5).
7. Each mismatch is one `opererr` refusal; all of them are joined. The same function feeds the doctor check.
8. Separately, `ValidateCustomSections` runs the original-tree validators before these steps: in `LoadConfig` at boot and reload, in `ze config validate`, and at editor commit. `validateOriginalTree` hands each validator the whole candidate tree, whichever root changed, and stops at the first validator that refuses. Two validators are added:
   - the firewall's `firewall-producer-tables` resolves the candidate's firewall backend (its leaf, or the schema default), runs every config table builder copp and firewall-irr registered over the tree, and runs that backend's registered verifier over the tables, joining every refusal (AC-8);
   - the VPP interface backend's `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` reads three things in the candidate tree: `interface backend`, the `interface wireguard` list and `vpp plugins wireguard`. With the backend `vpp`, one WireGuard interface or more, and the leaf not `true`, it refuses, naming every such interface in one refusal (AC-2). Because it reads the whole tree, a commit or reload that only changes the `vpp` root is judged like one that adds the interface.
9. One verdict, every door (C-5). Daemon start (`runYANGConfig`, and `RecoverConfig` for a rollback it loads), every reload (`diskConfigLoaders`) and a pushed candidate (`judgeConfigBeforeBoot`) call `LoadConfig`, which refuses. `judgeConfigBeforeBoot` hands it the `plugins` it holds, where today it hands `nil`, so the pushed candidate is parsed with the module text of the command-line plugins and resolved with them, as the boot after it is. The SSH editor, the web editor and web upload, the REST and gRPC sessions, gNMI Set, the SSH upload, the offline editing sessions and `ze config validate` reach `verifyTree` and `ValidateCustomSections`, which refuse with the same words; the SSH upload alone answers its fixed receipt in place of the words, as today, because a parser error can carry a secret, and its fix line names the validate command that prints them. Both `LoadConfig` and `verifyTree` take the command-line half of the selection from the one selection function (step 4), which reads the record and, in `loadConfig`, the parameter the start and reload already hand it, so no door can judge with another plugin set than its process runs. The ephemeral daemon of `ze config edit` is a door of its own process, started with the same `--plugin` arguments (step 4, consequence 4). Nothing tells the loader which door it serves, and no door keeps, reports or partly applies a configuration another door refuses.

### Boundaries Crossed
| Boundary | How | Verified |
|----------|-----|----------|
| Plugin or component to registry | Declaration at `init()`, a pure value | No |
| Registry to config component | `registry.All()` and the new accessors, as `ConfigNeeds` is read today | No |
| Config component to operator | `opererr` refusal; doctor diagnostic | No |
| copp and firewall-irr to firewall component | A config table builder registered from `init()`, run in process by the firewall's `firewall-producer-tables` tree validator, as `RegisterTables` is called in process at apply | No |
| Firewall component to config component | `config.RegisterTreeValidator("firewall-producer-tables", ...)` from `init()`, run by `ValidateCustomSections` | No |
| VPP interface backend to config component | `config.RegisterTreeValidator("iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin", ...)` from `init()` in `internal/plugins/iface/vpp`, which already imports the config component for its doctor checks; run by `ValidateCustomSections` | No |
| Candidate plugin selection to verdict | `ExtractPluginsFromTree` and `MergeCliPlugins`, both in the config component, in `LoadConfig` and in `verifyTree` | No |
| `ze` command line to every door of the process | `cmd/ze/ze_core_dispatch.go` records the `--plugin` list it parsed, once, through `config.RecordCommandLinePlugins`, before `zeDispatch` runs a verb; the selection function in the config component reads it. No root handler, constructor or hook carries the list | No |
| `ze config edit` to the ephemeral daemon it starts | `startEphemeralDaemon` (`internal/component/config/cli/cmd_edit.go`) reads the record and puts one global `--plugin <name>` pair per entry before `start` in the daemon's argv; that daemon's dispatcher records them as any `ze` process does | No |

### Changed-Wire Census

One inter-process carrier gains a writer, and one operator contract, the config
grammar, changes its values (N-1). The declarations are in-process registration
values read by the config component, as `ConfigNeeds` is. No RPC, event or
stored format changes, and no YANG leaf is added, renamed or removed.

The config grammar is the change N-1 makes: `interface backend`,
`firewall backend` and `traffic control backend` take `kernel` where they took
`netlink`, `nft` and `tc`, each schema default becomes `kernel`, and the
supporting list a `ze:backend` refusal prints names `kernel`. The writers are
every configuration an operator or the repository holds; the readers are the
three leaves, the verdict, the `ze:backend` walk and the PPPoE gate. Ze is
pre-release, so the old values are not accepted beside the new ones and no
conversion is written: a stored configuration that names one is refused at
start, commit, reload and `ze config validate`, with the fix that names
`kernel` (AC-19, R-11).

**The N-1 population is every whole-word occurrence, not what a phrase search
prints.** A hand list of the users of an old value was stale at every judging,
and so were the phrase searches that replaced it: the design judge of
2026-11-23 found users no phrase matched ("defaults to netlink", "the netlink
data plane", `LoadBackend("netlink")` quoted in a page, "backend (nft)", "back
to `netlink`", the heading "## tc Backend:", a capitalised "Backend"). A
pattern over prose cannot be complete, because prose can name a value in a
shape nobody listed. The population is therefore defined so that it is
complete by construction: the sweep `./le doc check retired-selector-values`
(AC-20) flags every whole-word occurrence of `netlink`, `nft` or `tc` in every
file git tracks, and an occurrence passes only when a declared exception covers
it. Every other occurrence is converted to `kernel`, or, where it names the
kernel mechanism in a shape no class covers, reworded or declared as a line
exception with its reason. The three retired values and their successor
`kernel` are declared once, in the sweep.

An occurrence is a run of letters, digits and underscores, bounded on each side
by a character that is none of those or by the edge of the line, that equals
`netlink`, `nft` or `tc` once folded to lower case. So `Netlink`, `TC`,
`"tc"`, `iface/netlink` and a back-quoted `nft` are occurrences, and
`nftables`, `rtnetlink`, `tcp`, `ifacenetlink` and `NETLINK_ROUTE` are not.
The sweep walks the tracked files through `walkTracked`
(`internal/le/doc/check/repository.go`), the walk the links and retired-command
sweeps share, so an untracked file is never read: the untracked drafts under
`test/draft/`, and `docs/features.md`, which `internal/le/feature` renders from
`features/*.md` and git ignores, so the sweep reads its sources. A file that
holds a NUL byte is skipped as binary, as `sweepRetired` skips one.

The class exceptions. Each is declared once with its rule and its reason: F-1
in `retiredRecords` (`internal/le/doc/check/retired.go`), which the sweep reads
and never copies, and every other class in the sweep, as `retiredWords` is in
`retired.go`. A file class is decided by the path alone,
an occurrence class by the occurrence and the characters around it, never by a
phrase list of what may name a backend. Classes are tried in this order, and
the count after each is the occurrences it covered first, measured 2026-11-23.

- F-1, history (1065, and 9 in owned journal rows, measured with the
  records under `plan/`, `website/` and `rfc/` and the two snapshot
  directories; the six other records, which join F-1 by this definition,
  hold 8 more, all in `internal/le/doc/check/testdata/doc_citation_baseline.txt`).
  The records `retiredRecords` (`internal/le/doc/check/retired.go`)
  declares, read from that array through the one function the
  retired-command sweep skips its records with. The sweep holds no path
  list of its own, so a record added to `retiredRecords` or removed from it
  joins or leaves F-1 in the same edit, and this spec names no record as
  the definition of F-1. A record is a file, a directory (a path ending in
  `/`, every file under it), or, after this work, a snapshot manifest (the
  files it pins, below). Each record states what was true on its day (a
  defect row, a closed-spec summary, a frozen handover, a recorder's break,
  an audit verdict, a published post or weekly, a captured log, page or
  record a test replays as captured, a verbatim copy of a past source), so
  a rewrite would falsify it.

  The reboot snapshots are recorded by their manifest. Today
  `retiredRecords` names four of them by path, the four that hold a retired
  command, each under `retiredRebootSnapshotWhy`. This sweep needs every
  snapshot skipped, because each is an inert source snapshot whose digest
  `plan/post-release/engineering/tooling-one-off-reboot-assets/manifest.json`
  records, so a rewrite breaks the digest (eight more of them hold 71
  occurrences, the test-case variable `tc` of a `.go.txt` copy among
  them). The manifest already declares which files are snapshots: each
  entry's `asset` whose `sha256` is set, and its `base_asset` whose
  `base_sha256` is set, each relative to the manifest's directory (140 and
  62 today, exactly the 140 tracked files under `unfinished/` and the 62
  under `base/`). `retired.go`'s record is a file or a directory matched by
  path alone, and neither shape fits: 202 file records would declare the
  manifest's pins a second time beside the file that declares them, and a
  snapshot pinned later would stay swept until someone copied its path
  (`ai/rules/principles.md`, "Every fact MUST be declared once"); a
  directory record for `base/` or `unfinished/` would also skip a file
  there that the manifest does not pin, which is live text and stays
  swept, as `TestRetiredCommandSweepSkipsHistoricalRecords` asserts with
  `base/internal/le/perf/live.go`. So the record gains one field, the
  manifest whose pinned files it covers, under `retiredRebootSnapshotWhy`,
  and the four snapshot path records are deleted in the same edit
  (`ai/rules/no-layering.md`); the retired-command sweep then skips every
  pinned snapshot too, for the reason it already gives the four. Each
  sweep reads the manifest once, through the tracked-file walk's root,
  before it walks. A manifest that is absent pins nothing, which is the
  true answer and sweeps more, never less (a fixture tree holds none). A
  manifest that cannot be read or parsed, an entry with a set digest and
  no path, or a pinned path that leaves the manifest's directory makes the
  sweep answer 2 with the manifest named as unreadable, never a record
  that covers nothing. The directory's `README.md`, the manifest itself
  and the `diagnostics/` subtree are not pinned, and stay swept by both
  sweeps.

  In every other spec under
  `plan/` (a file whose name starts `spec-` and ends `.md`) the owned
  journal rows section is skipped, from its exact heading to the next
  heading, as `retiredOwnedRowsAfter` skips it: its rows copy
  `plan/journal/` text verbatim, so a rewrite would make the copy disagree
  with the row it names. Every other path under `plan/` is swept: an open
  spec, a proposal and the spec template are not history, and each
  occurrence in one is a user converted in the same work (Repository users
  the refusals reach, U-20). This spec is covered by F-3, not here.
  `rfc/short/` is swept.
- F-2, not Ze's text (2122). A path under `vendor/`, `third_party/`,
  `rfc/full/`, `reference/`, `gokrazy/kernel/patches/` or
  `internal/le/vendorpatch/patches/`: vendored code, the RFC and
  Internet-Draft texts Ze copies, the Linux source the kernel patches change,
  and the patches to vendored modules (whose `package netlink` and test-case
  `tc` are the vendored library's). Ze does not write their words.
- F-3, the sweep itself and its design (501, all of them this spec).
  `internal/le/doc/check/retired_selector.go`, its test
  `retired_selector_test.go`, every file under
  `internal/le/doc/check/testdata/retired_selector/`, and this spec,
  `plan/release/fixes/spec-dataplane-compatibility-guard.md`. They declare the
  retired values and the shapes the sweep refuses, so they name them by
  necessity, as `retiredDeclarations` does for the retired-command sweep. The
  spec is one declared file, never a directory: no other spec under `plan/`
  is covered by it. Its entry is removed in the commit that closes the spec
  and deletes the file, so no exception outlives the path it names.
- F-4, the proofs of the refusal (0, files to create).
  `internal/component/config/dataplane_retired_values_test.go`,
  `test/parse/dataplane-refuses-old-backend-names.ci` and
  `test/plugin/dataplane-old-backend-names-refuse-start.ci`: the AC-19 tests
  write an old value to prove it is refused.
  `TestDataPlaneVerdictRefusesOldSelectorValues` lives in a file of its own so
  that the exception holds no other test.
- O-1, Go code (24873). In a `.go` file, an occurrence outside every comment
  and every string or rune literal, as `go/scanner` splits the file: an
  identifier such as the test-case variable `tc`, a package clause `package
  netlink`. A selector value reaches Go only as a string literal, and the
  comments say what the code does: both stay swept.
- O-2, a joined name (2040). The occurrence joined to a letter, digit or
  underscore by `/` or `.` on either side: a path
  (`internal/plugins/firewall/nft/`), a file or module name, an import path
  (`github.com/vishvananda/netlink`), a package qualifier (`netlink.LinkAdd`).
  A join by `-` alone is covered only when the word it sits in (the run of
  letters, digits, `_`, `-`, `.` and `/` around it) is a file or path name,
  holding a `/` or a `.` followed by a letter
  (`iface-netlink-accepts-x.ci`). It is part of a longer name, and the Go
  package names stay (N-1). Not covered: a hyphenated word that is not a file
  or path name ("netlink-only", "netlink-backed", "nft-based", the label
  "iface-netlink", "hw-tc-offload"), which names the backend or is declared as
  a line exception; and an occurrence joined by `/` or `-` to `vpp`, `kernel`
  or another retired value ("netlink/vpp", "nft/tc"), which lists selector
  values. The join partner is the run of letters, digits and underscores on
  the far side of that `/` or `-`, and it is compared after folding to lower
  case, as the occurrence is: `VPP`, `Vpp`, `Kernel`, `NFT` and `Netlink` are
  each a partner that lists selector values, so "tc/VPP", "VPP/netlink" and
  "nft/VPP" are refused, not covered as a path.
- O-3, all capitals (306). The occurrence written in capitals (`TC`, `NFT`,
  `NETLINK`): the DNS TC bit, the MPLS TC field, an acronym. A selector value
  is written in lower case, and YANG compares values by case. Not covered when
  the next word is `backend` or `backends`, in any case ("the TC backend"),
  nor when it is joined by `/` or `-` to a partner O-2 refuses ("TC/VPP",
  "NFT/kernel"), so a list of selector values in capitals is refused too.
- O-4, the traffic YANG module prefix (6). In a `.yang` file, the lower-case
  `tc` of a `prefix` statement: the keyword `prefix` at the start of the line
  or after `{` or `;`, then white space, `tc` and `;` (`prefix tc;`, also
  inside `import ... { prefix tc; }`); and, in any file, a lower-case `tc`
  directly followed by a colon and a letter (`tc:qdisc`). The prefix names the module,
  not a backend. Not covered: `prefix tc;` outside a `.yang` file, the word
  `prefix` before `tc` with no `;` after it (a description "the prefix tc
  names"), and `tc` followed by a colon and a space or a quote
  (`ze:backend "tc"`, "backend tc: rate").
- O-5, the kernel mechanism, by the next word (1222). The occurrence followed
  by one space and a word of a closed list, singular or plural. After
  `netlink`: socket, message, error, call, API, family, attribute, attr, field,
  library, handle, request, dump, subscription, multicast, monitor,
  monitoring, event, receive, round, round-trip, reply, write, read,
  operation, protocol, link, route, address, neighbor, neighbour,
  notification, update, deadline, timeout, failure. After `nft`: table, chain,
  rule, ruleset, set, expression, command, program, binary, tool, list, map,
  element, counter, flowtable, verdict, syntax, output, or an option word that
  starts with `-`. After `tc`: qdisc, class, filter, action, mirred, ingress,
  egress, sample, clsact, matchall, police, u32, flower, bpf, chain, monitor,
  command, program, binary, tool, or an option word. Each names the netlink
  socket and its objects, or the `nft` and `tc` programs and the kernel
  objects they manage. No word of the lists is `backend`, `interface`, `data`,
  `session`, `access` or `only`, and a selector value stands before one of
  those, before punctuation or at the end of a line.
- O-6, netlink after a mechanism word (280). `netlink`, in any case,
  preceded by one of `via`, `through`, `over`, `using`, `generic`, `raw` or
  `rt` and one space: "through netlink" and "generic netlink" name the
  socket. Not covered, whatever precedes it, when the occurrence is followed
  by `/`, or by a space, `or` or `and` and a space, and then `vpp` or
  `kernel` in any case ("via netlink or VPP", "through netlink/vpp",
  "using netlink and kernel"), which sets the kernel backend beside a plane;
  nor when either of the next two words after it starts with `backend`,
  `plane` or `default`, in any case, punctuation skipped ("via netlink
  backend", "via netlink data plane", whose next two words are "data" and
  "plane"; "using netlink (the default)"), which names the selected
  backend, its plane or its default.
- O-7, a program's argument vector (59). A string literal, in double or back
  quotes, that is exactly `nft` or `tc`, followed by a comma and a string
  literal that is a word of O-5's list for that program (`"nft", "list"`,
  `"tc", "qdisc"`): the argv of a program a fixture, a lab or a check runs.

The line exceptions. An occurrence no class covers and that does not name a
selector value is declared in the sweep with its file, the old value, the text
of the line that keeps it and the reason, as `retiredExceptions` is: a line is
covered when it holds the declared text, so another line of the same file that
names the value is still refused. Known members, each read 2026-11-23: the
doctor `Dependencies` entry `netlink` of gtsm, ospf and policyroute and the
kernel capability subsystem `netlink`, which name the socket; the `"tc"`
consumer arm of `internal/test/fixture/storage_consumer_restart.go`; the `tc`
DNS flag of `internal/test/dnsquery`; the acronym map entry `"tc": "TC"` of
`internal/le/site/plugins.go`; the command words of `monitor system netlink`;
and the fix text `dmesg | grep -i netlink` of
`internal/component/ike/engine/dynamic_wan.go`. Rewording mechanism prose
("nftables" for "nft", "the netlink socket" for a bare "netlink") is
preferred to a line exception wherever the sentence allows it.

Shapes a converted occurrence commonly takes, kept as illustrations of what the
sweep finds and defining nothing: a value after the word `backend` (config text
in block and `set` form in `.ci`, `.et`, `.wb`, `.conf`, Go fixtures, JSON and
the web handler goldens; "set firewall backend nft", "use backend nft",
"Required by backend tc", "backend (nft)"); a value that names a backend or a
plane ("the nft backend", "the netlink interface backend", "the netlink data
plane", "## tc Backend:", "netlink-only"); a value beside `vpp` ("netlink or
vpp", the annotation argument "netlink vpp"); a `ze:backend` argument; a
default (the YANG default of the traffic selector, "defaults to netlink",
"back to `netlink` (the default on Linux)"); and a Go string literal (a
registration name, a `LoadBackend` or `OpenBackend` argument, a `DataPlane`
value, a plane-keyed registry key, a `defaultBackendName` constant, a
comparison of a backend leaf with a value), quoted in a page as often as
written in Go.

A golden that echoes the YANG, such as
`internal/le/site/testdata/published-configuration.md` and
`internal/le/site/testdata/published-yang-config-tree.json`, is a tracked file
and is swept: it is taken again from its producer once the YANG it renders is
converted (U-18), and one that is not reddens the sweep.

Positive controls, each read 2026-11-23, are occurrences a correct sweep
refuses: `"set firewall backend nft"` in
`internal/plugins/anomaly/shape/doctor.go`; "the netlink interface backend
watches the address table" in
`internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_address_watch.go`; "Interface
management backend: netlink or vpp." in `ze-iface-conf.yang`; the `ze:backend
"netlink"` statements of `ze-vrrp-conf.yang`; the default of the traffic
selector in `ze-traffic-control-conf.yang`; `firewall.RegisterBackend("nft",
newBackend)` in `internal/plugins/firewall/nft/register.go` and `backend !=
"nft"` in `internal/plugins/firewall/nft/doctor_linux.go`; and the judge's
finds below. Negative controls, occurrences a correct sweep passes: the
package path `internal/plugins/firewall/nft/` (O-2), the test-case variable
`tc` in Go code (O-1), the DNS `TC` bit (O-3), "the netlink socket" (O-5) and
`"nft", "list"` in a fixture's argv (O-7).

Positive controls the narrowed classes refuse, each read 2026-11-23 and
each followed by the class that covered it before it was narrowed: "via
netlink or VPP backend" in `docs/DESIGN.md` lines 369 and 372, "via netlink
or VPP" in `docs/plugin-overview.md` line 216 and `docs/features/plugins.md`
line 93 (O-6); "tc/VPP" in `website/compare/nos.md` line 111,
`internal/component/traffic/cli/register.go` lines 7 and 62 and
`test/ui/root-namespace.ci` line 14, "VPP/netlink" in
`website/compare/nos.md` line 294 and "nft/VPP" in the same page, line 311
(O-2); and the open specs `plan/post-release/features/spec-traffic-ingress-shaping.md`
line 252 (`backend tc` in a fix), `plan/release/fixes/spec-firewall-vpp-apply-before-connect.md`
line 114 (`firewall { backend nft; }`) and
`plan/post-release/features/spec-firewall-fragment-match.md` line 57
(`ze:backend "nft"`) (F-1). Each is a user (U-20, U-21).

Measured 2026-11-23 over the working tree at `e6a22cc73e` (46334 tracked
files), with a throwaway script that applies the occurrence rule and the
class rules above, F-1 narrowed to the `retiredRecords` records under
`plan/`, `website/` and `rfc/` and the two snapshot directories (the six

define nothing: 36356 whole-word occurrences in all; covered by F-1 1065 and

value are in `ze-iface-conf.yang`, `ze-firewall-conf.yang`,

  (`PluginForDataPlane("netlink")`, `autoLoadNames(..., "netlink")` and their
  messages); `docs/architecture/iface/logical-name-resolution.md` line 409
  (`iface.LoadBackend("netlink")`); six comments of `test/plugin` `.ci` files
  that name the netlink data plane or its default; `docs/guide/firewall.md`
  line 17 (the source anchor `RegisterBackend("nft")`);
  `docs/guide/policy-routing.md` line 326 ("The firewall backend (nft)"); the
  headings of
  `docs/guide/traffic-control.md` ("## tc Backend: TBF Root Rate", "## tc
  Backend: Original Qdisc Snapshot", "## tc Backend: Filter Priorities"); and
  `docs/features/interfaces.md` line 1863 ("Use netlink elsewhere"), together
  with its feature source under `features/`.

The phrase searches used before this section (bare literals, then
comparisons and operator strings, then page phrases, then the six shapes S1 to
S6 of an earlier version) each missed a shape the next judge found. The sweep
replaces them, and the shapes above only illustrate it.

The ExaBGP migration writes no backend leaf (no hit under `internal/exabgp`).

The carrier that gains a writer is the `ze` argv itself. `startEphemeralDaemon`
(`internal/component/config/cli/cmd_edit.go`) builds the argv of the daemon
`ze config edit` starts when none runs, today `<exe> start [--web <port>]
[--insecure-web] <path>`, and execs the same binary with it. It gains one
global `--plugin <name>` pair per recorded entry, before `start`. The reader is
the new process's `zeParseGlobalFlags` (`cmd/ze/ze_core_dispatch.go`), which
already parses a global `--plugin` before the verb (its `--plugin` case appends
the value to `zeFlags.plugins`) and is not changed. Writer and reader are the
same binary, so no version skew exists between them.

**Argv search evidence:** scope `cmd` and `internal`, excluding `_test.go`;
term `os.Executable()` (searched 2026-11-23), each hit read for what it runs.
Two shipped callers re-exec the `ze` binary to start a daemon:
`startEphemeralDaemon` (argv `<exe> start ...`), and `forkAndServe`
(`internal/plugins/provision/main.go`, argv `<exe> -`, with a configuration
`ze provision` generates written on stdin, served by `setupDispatch` in
`cmd/ze/setup_dispatch.go` or by the `-` branch of `zeDispatch`).
`forkAndServe` is not changed: it forwards no `--plugin` today, the
configuration its daemon judges is the one provisioning generated, not a
candidate the operator edits, and no editor session commits through that
daemon, so that daemon judges with the empty list its own argv names. Every
other shipped hit reads the executable path and starts no `ze` daemon:
`update-cmd` hashes the binary, `local` and `systemd` resolve it for
installation, `selfupdate` replaces it, `paths` and `childenv` locate the
install directory, and the doctor storage check reads it. The hits under
`internal/le`, `internal/test` and `internal/chaos` are tooling and test
re-execs, outside the shipped binary. Positive control for the producer and
the consumer: `cmd_edit.go` holds `argv = append(argv, exe, "start")`, the one
append of `"start"` to an argv under `cmd`, `internal/component` and
`internal/plugins` (search of `"start"` on lines that also name `append`,
`argv`, `args` or `exec`, outside tests), and
`zeParseGlobalFlags` holds `case "--plugin":`.

The SSH configuration upload receipt is not a wire this spec changes. Its fix
line gains the daemon's `--plugin` arguments (AC-18), but no program reads the
refusal text: `internal/appliance/cmd_config_push.go`, the client that reads
the receipt, judges only the channel's exit status and whether the output
equals `configupload.Receipt`, and on a nonzero status answers with a refusal
of its own; the one other reader, the deployment test
`internal/le/test/qemu/install_config_push_ssh.go`, looks only for
`configupload.Receipt`. The success receipt and the exit status are unchanged,
so a fleet tool and a device on the previous image keep the push protocol the
appliance door names. The fix line is text for the operator.

**Search evidence:** scope `internal/component/plugin/registry`, `pkg/plugin/rpc`,
`pkg/plugin/sdk`, `internal/component/config`; terms `ConfigNeeds`, `DataPlane`,
`NeedsDataPlane`.
Positive control: `ConfigNeeds` is found in `internal/component/plugin/registry/registry.go`,
in the iface, firewall, traffic and flow export registrations and in
`internal/component/config/optional_provider.go`.
Result for the public packages (searched 2026-11-23): `pkg/plugin/rpc` and
`pkg/plugin/sdk` hold no occurrence of any of the three terms, and the whole of
`pkg/plugin` holds none of `ConfigNeeds`. No Stage 1 type carries a config
need or a data plane today, so the new declarations add nothing to the wire.
Assumption A-6 covers what that means for an external plugin.

| `ze` argv global flags | reader | `cmd/ze/ze_core_dispatch.go` `zeParseGlobalFlags`, the `--plugin` case: unchanged, parses each global `--plugin` before the verb into `zeFlags.plugins`, which the dispatcher then records | Positive control `case "--plugin":`, read 2026-11-23 | preserved: no reader change; writer and reader are the same binary |

| SSH upload receipt | reader | `internal/appliance/cmd_config_push.go`: reads the exit status and compares the output with `configupload.Receipt`; reads no refusal text. `internal/le/test/qemu/install_config_push_ssh.go` looks only for `configupload.Receipt` | Read 2026-11-23 | preserved: neither reader parses the fix line, so the receipt is not a changed wire |

### Integration Points
- `internal/component/config/optional_provider.go` `refuseAbsentOptionalProviders` - the new verdict runs beside it, at both of its doors.
- `registry.DataPlaneWriter` - unchanged; its data plane argument comes from the same selector declaration.
- `iface.RegisterBackend`, `firewall.RegisterBackend`, `traffic.RegisterBackend` - unchanged signatures; the kernel backends register as `kernel`, so each registered name is the plane (N-1, AC-11).
- `config.RegisterTreeValidator` - the firewall component registers `firewall-producer-tables`, which runs every registered config table builder (copp's `translatePolicy`, the IRR interface-binding builder) over the candidate tree and `firewall.RunVerifier` for the candidate's firewall backend over the result. The VPP interface backend registers `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` (AC-2).
- `config.ExtractPluginsFromTree`, `config.MergeCliPlugins` - the verdict reads the candidate's plugin selection through them, for a declaring plugin with no config root (AC-3, flowspec-firewall), at start and at `ze config validate` alike.
- `config.RecordCommandLinePlugins`, new, beside `RecordStartedPlugins` in `internal/component/config/declared_schemas.go` - the `ze` dispatcher records the `--plugin` list once; the selection function reads it at every door, beside `loadConfig`'s own parameter (Q-2, AC-18). Its other readers display it (the upload receipt) or forward it (`startEphemeralDaemon`).
- `diagnostic.RegisterDoctorCheck` - one new check owned by the config component.

### Architectural Verification
| Check | Holds? | Evidence |
|-------|--------|----------|
| No bypassed layers (data flows through the intended path) | Yes | The verdict runs where `ConfigNeeds` is judged |
| No unintended coupling (components stay isolated) | Yes | Config reads the registry only; no import of iface, firewall, traffic or any feature. The producer table check lives in the firewall component and reaches the config doors as a registered tree validator. `refuseUnresolvableDependencies` is unchanged: it still does not resolve the FIB writer, and only its stated reason (config cannot read the data plane) stops holding |
| No duplicated functionality (extends existing, does not recreate) | Yes | The walker is reused; four call sites collapse to one |
| Zero-copy preserved where applicable (refs, not copies) | Yes | Not a wire path |
| Registration over hardcoding, outbound | Yes | Every plane and selector is declared by its owner |
| Registration over hardcoding, inbound | Yes | Lists searched for feature names: `validatedSections` in `internal/component/config/validate_sections.go`, the `checkBackends` defaults, the `validateGroups` backend constant. The last two are removed; the first is not extended |

### Risks
| ID | Risk | Early signal | Mitigation / fallback |
|----|------|--------------|----------------------|
| R-1 | A shipped config, `.ci` body, fixture, deployment scenario or interop scenario holds a combination this spec refuses, or asserts a doctor code it removes, and now fails | `./le test traffic`, `./le test functional plugin`, `./le test functional parse`, `./le test functional vrrp`, `./le test deployment vpp` reds | Every user found is listed with its conversion in Repository users the refusals reach (Files to Modify), and each is converted in the phase that makes the refusal, as `ai/META-DECISIONS.md` requires of a change to what Ze does. A conversion that changes a test's input, expected output or fixture so it stays green is judged by an independent agent running `ze-test-judge` before it lands, and the commit carries its `Weakening-approved:` line |
| R-2 | A VPP box loses kernel-side protection of its own control plane | Operator report; C-1 forecloses it knowingly | Recorded in Known Limitations |
| R-3 | The engine walk judges a node against the wrong selector when a module outside every family root is annotated | A refusal naming the wrong leaf to change | The rule is fixed in Data Flow step 5; a unit test covers the container network node, the one annotated node outside every family root. The VRRP container sits inside the interface root and is judged as today |
| R-4 | The verdict and the doctor row drift apart | Different words in the two outputs | One function produces both, as `DataPlaneWriter` does |
| R-5 | Reload adds a root whose plugin is not running, so its in-use predicate is not evaluated | A reload that accepts what a start refuses | Predicates read the tree and the registry, never a running plugin; `test/reload/dataplane-mismatch-reload-refused.ci` |
| R-6 | Another session changes the same VPP files | Merge noise in `internal/plugins/firewall/vpp`, `internal/plugins/static` | Coordinate with the specs in Blast Radius |
| R-7 | OSPF authentication through the XFRM seam (RFC 4552) is read as IPsec in use | An OSPF config refused on a VPP box for IPsec | The IPsec predicate reads the IPsec config root only. Whether OSPF's own XFRM use works on VPP is checked at its producer before any declaration is added for it |
| R-8 | A box whose stored configuration holds a mismatch today does not boot after the upgrade that carries this spec, IPsec under `vpp` included | `ze` exits at start with the refusal | Thomas's decision (C-5 (a)). The refusal names the fix, the new release's `ze config validate` gives the same answer before the upgrade, and `ze doctor` reports the row on the stored file with no daemon. Recorded in Known Limitations |

| R-9 | The command-line plugins are one fact held twice in a daemon: the `cliPlugins` parameter of `LoadConfig`, which decides what starts, and the record, which serves the doors that hold no list | A caller hands `LoadConfig` a list and no record exists, and the verdict judges without it. The case exists today: the `le chaos run` in-process simulation (`internal/chaos/inprocess/runner.go`) hands `["ze.bgp-rs"]` through `LoadReactorWithPluginsStandalone` in a process the `ze` dispatcher never ran. The reverse case exists too: `ze doctor` and the managed push handler hand `nil` in a process whose record holds a list | The verdict inside `loadConfig` reads its own `cliPlugins` parameter: the selection function merges the tree's `plugin` blocks with the record and with the parameter its caller hands it, and `MergeCliPlugins` keeps one entry per resolved name. A caller that holds a list is judged with it, record or none (the chaos runner); a caller that passes `nil` is judged with the record (`ze doctor`, the push handler); in a daemon both are `zeFlags.plugins`, parsed in one place, and `TestDispatcherRecordsTheCommandLinePlugins` proves the dispatcher records the slice it hands `cmdStart`, so the union is that slice. `verifyTree` holds no list and reads the record alone. `TestLoadConfigVerdictReadsItsCommandLinePlugins` proves the chaos runner's case: with an empty record, `LoadConfig` handed `ze.flowspec-firewall` refuses the mismatch. Every other reader of the record only displays or forwards it |
| R-10 | The record is process-wide state, and a test that writes it leaks into the next test | A verdict test that passes alone and fails in a package run | The judging function takes the selection as an argument, so its tests write no record. The few tests that write one (the selection function, the dispatcher, `ze config validate`) restore the previous value in `t.Cleanup` and do not run in parallel, as a registry test restores `registry.Snapshot()` |
| R-11 | A configuration that names `netlink`, `nft` or `tc` is refused after N-1: a repository user left unconverted goes red, and an operator's stored configuration stops a daemon from booting after the upgrade | A `.ci`, fixture or deployment red naming the selector; `ze` exiting at start with the AC-19 refusal | The population is every whole-word occurrence of an old value in a tracked file that no declared exception covers (Changed-Wire Census), complete by construction rather than by a phrase list, and rows U-14 to U-21 convert it in the same commit as the rename, so no partial rename lands. AC-20 proves it empty after the work: `./le doc check retired-selector-values` refuses every such occurrence, today's and any written later. The refusal names the selector, says the value names no backend this build registers, and gives `set <selector> kernel`, read from the schema default. Ze is pre-release: no alias and no conversion (Known Limitations) |

## Blast Radius

| Question | Answer |
|----------|--------|
| What breaks if this is wrong? | A valid configuration is refused at commit or at start, so a daemon does not boot after an upgrade. Nothing on the wire changes. |
| How is it reverted? | Single commit revert per phase. No stored format changes, but the config grammar does: N-1 changes the values the three selector leaves accept and their defaults (Changed-Wire Census). Reverting the N-1 commit reverts the grammar and every repository configuration it converted together, and a configuration an operator wrote with `kernel` in between is then refused by the reverted build until it names `netlink`, `nft` or `tc` again. |
| Who else touches this path? | `plan/release/fixes/spec-firewall-vpp-apply-before-connect.md` (skeleton; Task read): owns when a VPP backend's apply waits for VPP, for the firewall, traffic and static backends. It shares no AC with H or K: it owns apply timing, this spec owns the judgment of copp's and the IRR bindings' tables before apply, by the `firewall-producer-tables` validator. It does share G: its Task names the static plugin choosing its backend once, before the handshake, so AC-7 here makes the configuration choose the plane and leaves the not-yet-connected behavior to that spec. `plan/release/fixes/spec-dataplane-seams-2-backend-typed-index.md`: makes the kernel index and the VPP index distinct in `iface.Resolve`; A and the sampling half of E remove the configurations that reach the mismatch, that spec removes the type confusion. `plan/release/validation/spec-kernel-capability-gate.md` (blocked): same verdict shape for the host kernel. `plan/release/fixes/spec-explicit-plugin-and-attach-config.md` (Depends, coordination): replaces the `plugin internal` and `plugin external` blocks with `bus { endpoint }` and owns the query-mode declaration of an external program with no daemon. The verdict reads the selection through `ExtractPluginsFromTree` and `MergeCliPlugins`, which that spec rewires, so whichever spec lands second converts the other's `.ci` bodies to the grammar then in force; neither changes what the other judges. `plan/release/fixes/spec-vpp-nat44-pool-ownership.md`, `plan/release/fixes/spec-vpp-reply-deadline-iface-fib-static.md`, `plan/release/fixes/spec-fib-vpp-enabled-and-batching-leaves.md`: same packages as F, G and A; not read, read each before editing. `plan/post-release/features/spec-vrrp-7-vpp.md`, `plan/post-release/features/spec-pppoe-vpp-dataplane.md`, `plan/post-release/features/spec-ipsec-vpp-dataplane-readback.md`, `plan/post-release/features/spec-tunnel-outer-ttl-on-the-vpp-backend.md`: each adds a VPP implementation and then changes one declaration or annotation. |

## Wiring Test (MANDATORY -- NOT deferrable)

| Entry Point | → | Feature Code | Test |
|-------------|---|--------------|------|
| `ze config validate` on a file with `interface backend vpp` and a `firewall` block on `nft` | → | data-plane verdict in `verifyTree` | `test/parse/dataplane-vpp-refuses-kernel-firewall.ci` |
| Daemon start with the same file | → | same verdict, in `LoadConfig` | `test/plugin/dataplane-mismatch-refuses-start.ci` |
| Daemon start with `vpn ipsec` under `interface backend vpp` | → | same verdict, in `LoadConfig`, with no start exception for IPsec | `test/plugin/dataplane-vpp-ipsec-refuses-start.ci` |
| Reload of a running `netlink` daemon with a kernel firewall, changing only `interface backend` to `vpp` | → | same verdict, in `LoadConfig` | `test/reload/dataplane-mismatch-reload-refused.ci` |
| `ze doctor` on the file of row 1 | → | doctor check calling the same function | `test/plugin/dataplane-mismatch-doctor.ci` |
| `ze config validate` with a `plugin internal` block using `flowspec-firewall`, `interface backend vpp`, no `firewall backend vpp` | → | the verdict's "candidate selects this plugin" form of in use | `test/parse/dataplane-vpp-refuses-flowspec-firewall-without-vpp-firewall.ci` |
| `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall config validate` on a file with `interface backend vpp`, no `plugin` block and no `firewall backend vpp`, then a daemon start on the same file with the same `--plugin` | → | `--plugin` recorded by the dispatcher and merged by the selection function; the same verdict at both doors | `test/plugin/dataplane-validate-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| A daemon started with `--plugin ze.flowspec-firewall` on a `kernel` file; an SSH editor session sets `interface backend vpp` and commits | → | the SSH editor's validator, `verifyTree`, the recorded command-line plugins | `test/plugin/dataplane-door-ssh-editor-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| The same daemon; the web editor commits the same change | → | `validateTransition` in the web editor, `verifyTree`, the record | `test/plugin/dataplane-door-web-editor-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| The same daemon; a REST config session commits the same change | → | `configValidationHook`, `verifyTree`, the record | `test/plugin/dataplane-door-api-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| The same daemon; a gNMI Set carries the same change | → | the same hook, set by `buildGNMISessionManager` | `test/plugin/dataplane-door-gnmi-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| The same daemon; an SSH config upload replaces the file with one that sets `interface backend vpp` | → | `configMutationOwner.Upload`, `ValidateContent`, `verifyTree`, the record | `test/plugin/dataplane-door-upload-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall start` with a pushed candidate that sets `interface backend vpp` waiting, then the same candidate pushed to the running managed daemon | → | `judgeConfigBeforeBoot`, `LoadConfig` with the command-line plugins; the `managed.Handler` `Validate` closure, `ReadConfig` with `nil`, the record | `test/managed/dataplane-door-pushed-config-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall config edit` on a `kernel` file with no daemon running; the session sets `interface backend vpp` and commits | → | `runStoredEditor`, `startEphemeralDaemon` passing the recorded `--plugin` before `start`, the ephemeral daemon's dispatcher recording it, its SSH editor's validator, `verifyTree` | `test/plugin/dataplane-door-ephemeral-editor-sees-command-line-plugin.ci` |
| `ze config validate` with `interface backend vpp` and a `fib kernel` block, no `fib vpp` block | → | the verdict's FIB writer rule | `test/parse/dataplane-vpp-refuses-fib-kernel-without-fib-vpp.ci` |
| `ze config validate` with a WireGuard interface under `vpp` and `vpp plugins wireguard` absent | → | `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` tree validator, run by `ValidateCustomSections` | `test/parse/iface-vpp-wireguard-needs-plugin.ci` |
| Daemon start with the same file | → | the same validator, in `LoadConfig` | `test/plugin/iface-vpp-wireguard-needs-plugin-refuses-start.ci` |
| Reload that changes only `vpp plugins wireguard` from `true` to `false` | → | the same validator, over the whole candidate tree | `test/reload/vpp-wireguard-plugin-off-reload-refused.ci` |
| `ze config validate` on an annotated node | → | engine-owned `ze:backend` walk | `test/parse/iface-vpp-rejects-bridge.ci` (existing, unchanged) |
| Daemon start with a copp configuration under `firewall backend vpp` | → | `firewall-producer-tables` tree validator, run by `ValidateCustomSections` in `LoadConfig` | `test/plugin/firewall-vpp-copp-table-refuses-start.ci` |
| `ze config validate` with an IRR interface binding under `firewall backend vpp` | → | the same validator, through the IRR builder | `test/parse/firewall-vpp-irr-interface-binding-refused-at-commit.ci` |
| `ze config validate` on a file with `interface backend netlink`, `firewall backend nft` and `traffic control backend tc`, then a daemon start on the same file | → | the verdict's refusal of a selector value no registered backend answers, in `verifyTree` and `LoadConfig` | `test/parse/dataplane-refuses-old-backend-names.ci`, `test/plugin/dataplane-old-backend-names-refuse-start.ci` |

Each of the seven door rows (the five daemon doors, the pushed candidate and the
ephemeral editor) uses a candidate that only the command-line plugin makes a
mismatch: `interface backend vpp` with no `firewall` block is accepted when
nothing selects a table producer (AC-3 survivors). Each asserts the answer that
door's own validation gives, naming the `--plugin` argument, and that the stored
configuration is unchanged (A-16).

## Acceptance Criteria

| AC ID | Input / Condition | Expected Behavior | Permitted Survivors and Why |
|-------|-------------------|-------------------|-----------------------------|
| AC-1 | `fib vpp` with `interface backend kernel` (A) | Commit, start and validate refuse, naming `fib vpp` and the fix `set interface backend vpp` | `fib vpp` with `interface backend vpp` commits and installs routes: the writer's plane matches. `fib kernel` with `interface backend kernel` commits. A FIB plugin that declares no data plane (`fib-p4`) is not judged: it has declared nothing to compare. `fib kernel` beside `fib vpp` with `interface backend vpp` commits, and `fib kernel` alone there is refused: AC-14 |
| AC-2 | A `wireguard` interface under `interface backend vpp` with `vpp plugins wireguard` not true (B) | Commit, start, reload and validate refuse, naming every such interface and the fix `set vpp plugins wireguard true`. The check is the original-tree validator `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin`, registered by the VPP interface backend from `init()`; it reads `interface backend`, the `interface wireguard` list and `vpp plugins wireguard` in the whole candidate tree. A commit or reload that only sets `vpp plugins wireguard` to `false`, or deletes it, on a configuration that has such an interface is refused the same way: every door hands the validator the whole tree, whichever root changed (A-12). The doctor check `vpp-wireguard-plugin` calls the same function | The same interface with the leaf true commits; any WireGuard interface under `kernel` commits: the kernel needs no VPP plugin. A build without `ze_vpp` holds no such validator and needs none: it already refuses `interface backend vpp` through `ConfigNeeds`. Today the mismatch at daemon start fails in the interface apply; this AC moves the refusal ahead of any plugin start, with the fix line |
| AC-3 | `interface backend vpp` with a `firewall` block on backend `kernel`, a `traffic control` block on backend `kernel`, explicit or by default, or a firewall table producer in use with no `firewall backend vpp` (C) | Commit, start and validate refuse, naming the block or the producer's root and the fix `set firewall backend vpp` or `set traffic control backend vpp`. The producers and their in-use predicates, each declared by its owner: copp (`control-plane-protection` present), ddos-local and anomaly shape (their config roots present), flowspec-firewall (the candidate selects the plugin: a `plugin internal` or `plugin external` block whose `use` leaf names it, or a `--plugin` argument; it owns no config root, A-10), policy routing (judged by AC-5). The flowspec-firewall refusal names the `plugin` block that selects it and two fixes: `set firewall backend vpp`, or `delete` of that block; selected by `--plugin`, it names the argument and the fixes `set firewall backend vpp` or starting without that `--plugin`. `ze config validate` judges with the `--plugin` arguments the daemon takes: the `ze` dispatcher records them before the verb runs, and the verdict merges the record through `MergeCliPlugins` as `finalizeConfigLoad` does, so a plugin named there is judged at validate as at start, at reload and at every commit door (Q-2, AC-18). A compiled-in plugin's declaration is read from the registry; an external program's is obtained as `spec-explicit-plugin-and-attach-config` defines. firewall-irr and firewall-domain own the `firewall` root, so the `firewall` block already carries them. The VRRP filters and the IKE source guard run only with features already refused under `vpp` (the VRRP annotation, AC-5), and GTSM is judged by AC-5; none of the three declares a producer. The netlink rpf table is written by the netlink interface backend itself, and the container plugin reaches the firewall by RPC with its network list refused under `vpp`: neither is a registry producer | `kernel` for all three selectors commits unchanged. `vpp` with `firewall backend vpp` and `traffic control backend vpp` commits. A `vpp` config with no firewall block, no traffic control block and no table producer in use commits: an unused default selects nothing. flowspec-firewall selected under `vpp` with `firewall backend vpp` commits, and its tables are judged in `ApplyAll` when a FlowSpec route produces them (AC-8 survivors). A plugin block under another key that uses `flowspec-firewall` is judged like one keyed by its name: the `use` value decides. flowspec-firewall named only by `--plugin` is refused at validate, start and reload with the same words; the same file validated without that `--plugin` is accepted, because then nothing selects the plugin, as a start without it selects none |
| AC-4 | `firewall backend vpp` or `traffic control backend vpp` with `interface backend kernel` (D) | Commit, reload, validate and start refuse (start by AC-16), naming the leaf and both fixes: change the leaf, or `set interface backend vpp` | The matching pairs of AC-3 commit |
| AC-5 | `interface backend vpp` with IPsec, L2TP, flow export sampling, policy routing or GTSM in use (E, J). GTSM is in use when a BGP peer or group sets `ttl max`, `ttl set` or `ttl min` to a nonzero value, the condition under which `gtsm.SetPeers` writes kernel routes or the kernel table. L2TP is in use when an `environment l2tp server` entry exists and `l2tp enabled` is not `false`: a listener is what starts the kernel worker that calls `prepareKernelBackend` (`subsystem.go`), and the `l2tp` block alone also carries the subscriber authentication and pool PPPoE shares (U-11) | Commit, start and validate refuse, one refusal per feature, naming the feature's config path, saying it programs the Linux kernel only, with the fix `set interface backend kernel`; the GTSM refusal also names `delete` of the peer's `ttl` leaf as the second fix. IPsec is refused at start like the others (AC-16) | Each of the five commits under `kernel`. Under `vpp`: flow export with neither the sampling container nor conntrack flow records (the conntrack records are AC-15), PPPoE access with a VPP provider, beside an `l2tp` block that holds no listener (`internal/le/test/deployment/vpppppoe/product_plan.go`), BGP peers with no nonzero `ttl` leaf and the other routing protocols commit, because none declares a kernel forwarding plane. A `sysctl` block commits under `vpp`, every key of it: `sysctl` declares the kernel plane and sets the host-side mark on its own registration, the second host-side use the decision row allows (Q-1 (b): "so we can consider mix setup when we add vrf"). Its keys are applied to the Linux kernel exactly as on any data plane, so a setting about kernel behaviour works unchanged; only a key about packet forwarding, such as `rp_filter` or `forwarding`, acts on the packets that reach Linux rather than the packets VPP forwards, which is documented in Known Limitations and on the generated page, not refused. GTSM is refused under `vpp` because both of its writers (`installHopLimitRoute`, `publishFilter`) program the kernel and nothing reaches VPP; the decision row allows no host-side meaning to any feature but `fib kernel` and `sysctl` |
| AC-6 | `snat` or `masquerade` term with a source address match under `firewall backend vpp` (F) | Commit refuses, naming table, chain and term, and says VPP NAT44 applies to the whole inside interface | The same term without the match commits on VPP; the same term with the match commits on `kernel`, which applies it |
| AC-7 | A static route in a named table (G) | The static plugin chooses its route backend from the data plane the configuration selects, read through the declared selector, never from whether a VPP connector exists. Under `vpp` no named-table route is written to the kernel; a VPP channel that fails to open is an error with a doctor code, not a kernel fallback | Under `kernel` the kernel write is unchanged. A static route in the main table keeps its Loc-RIB path on both planes. While VPP is not yet connected, the static backend waits, defers or retries as `plan/release/fixes/spec-firewall-vpp-apply-before-connect.md` decides for all three VPP backends: that spec's Task already owns the static backend's choice before the handshake |
| AC-8 | A `control-plane-protection` (copp) configuration, or an IRR interface binding, under `firewall backend vpp` (H, K) | Commit, start, reload and validate refuse with the VPP verifier's message; at start and reload the refusal comes before any plugin starts or changes. copp and firewall-irr each register a config table builder with the firewall component from `init()` (copp: `translatePolicy`; irr: an interface-binding table builder answering, for each binding in the candidate, the table shape it produces once its list holds prefixes, the ingress chain and its named set, whatever is stored, since the verifier judges a named set by its shape and `buildIfaceTables` emits nothing for a binding with no prefixes). A firewall term naming an IRR AS-SET or a domain group needs no builder: the firewall's own verifier already refuses its `MatchInSet` at commit. The firewall component registers one original-tree validator, `firewall-producer-tables`, which resolves the candidate's firewall backend (its leaf, or the schema default), runs every registered builder over the candidate tree and runs that backend's verifier over the result, joining every refusal. `ValidateCustomSections` runs it in `LoadConfig` at boot and reload, in `ze config validate` and at editor commit, which is how it reaches daemon start: an `InProcessConfigVerifier` alone would not (`yang-config-design.md`, Layer 3) | copp and IRR bindings under `firewall backend kernel` commit and apply. Today, at daemon start, H and K already stop startup when `ApplyAll` refuses the table; this AC moves that refusal ahead of any plugin start, with the operator's words and the fix line. Policy routing is not judged here: with `firewall backend vpp` it is refused by AC-4 under `kernel` and by AC-5 under `vpp`. ddos-local, flowspec-firewall and anomaly shape build their tables only at run time, so no commit holds them: they stay judged in `ApplyAll`, and their use under `vpp` with a kernel firewall is refused by AC-3 |
| AC-9 | Any refusal of AC-1, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-14, AC-15 or AC-19, and any refusal of the engine walk of AC-10 | `ze doctor` reports the same problem, why and fix under one doctor code, `doctor-dataplane-mismatch`, from the same function as the refusal. The walk's refusals join it because the VRRP hand check and its code `doctor-vrrp-backend-unusable` are deleted (Existing Mechanisms, Hand checks and Doctor checks): a VRRP group under `vpp`, refused by its `ze:backend "kernel"` annotation (`"netlink"` today), keeps a doctor row under this code (U-12) | A matching configuration produces no row: an absent mismatch is not reported. AC-2's refusal keeps its own code, `doctor-vpp-wireguard`, whose check calls the same function as the `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` tree validator. A daemon refused at start reports the row through `ze doctor` on the stored file, with no daemon running |
| AC-10 | A `ze:backend` node anywhere in the config tree | One walk in the config doors refuses it with today's wording; iface, firewall, traffic and the container plugin call no gate of their own. The walk judges the tree as the operator wrote it, before any schema default is materialized (`docs/features/interfaces.md`, "Outer-header TTL") | Every node accepted today stays accepted: the walk changes no annotation (the three firewall annotations of case I are AC-17). A VPP-backed `tunnel` that names no `ttl` commits, although its `ttl` leaf is `ze:backend "kernel"` and carries a default |
| AC-11 | A backend registered with a component | The name a backend registers under is the data plane it programs, `kernel` or `vpp` (N-1), and `RegisterBackend` keeps its signature: no second plane argument declares the same fact. The data planes are the names the data-plane selector's backends register (the interface backends); a unit test over the composition root proves that every firewall and traffic backend name, every `DataPlane` a FIB plugin declares, every PPPoE session provider key and every MPLS forwarding reader key is one of them | The `noop`, `recording`, `xfrm` and `vpp` backends of the IKE dataplane registry are outside the three families and owe none: the IKE engine registration declares the IPsec plane |
| AC-12 | `docs/guide/dataplane-features.md` | The page is generated from the `ze:backend` annotations, the backend registrations and the feature declarations; the doc check refuses a stale copy; the hand-written page is deleted first | The verifier limit tables stay hand-written on the firewall and traffic guides, with source anchors: the verifier rules are code |
| AC-13 | A reload that changes only `interface backend` from `kernel` to `vpp` on a config with a kernel-only feature (the test uses a `firewall` block on `kernel`) | The reload is refused and the running configuration stays active | A reload that changes the backend and removes or converts the feature in the same commit succeeds. A reload is refused for IPsec too, by the same verdict as the start |
| AC-14 | A `fib kernel` block with `interface backend vpp`, beside a `fib vpp` block (C-3 (a)) and alone (P-1 (b)) | Beside `fib vpp`, it commits, starts, reloads and validates. The mechanism is the host-side mark of Data Flow step 6, set by `fib-kernel` in its own registration: the declaration acts on the Linux host itself, beside the selected data plane, and for `fib-kernel` that is programming the Linux route table for the router's own traffic. The verdict reads the mark and reports no plane mismatch for a declaration that carries it; no engine or config code names a FIB plugin. Alone, with no `fib vpp` block, it is refused at commit, start, reload and validate, for now (Thomas: "atm"): "fib kernel programs the Linux route table only, and interface backend is "vpp" / VPP forwards the packets, and with no fib vpp block its FIB gets no route / `set fib vpp`". The rule reads the mark, `ProgramsFIB` and `DataPlane` of the in-use FIB plugins: a marked writer of another plane with no writer of the selected plane beside it is refused (an unmarked one is already refused by AC-1, and is not refused twice), and the fix names the config root of the registration that declares `ProgramsFIB` and the selected plane, so the fix line is derived, never a literal. `fibPluginChosen` and `appendDataPlaneWriter` are unchanged: an explicit `fib` block still always wins, and Ze adds no writer the operator did not write | `fib kernel` under `kernel` commits and stays the one writer; `fib vpp` under `vpp` commits; `fib kernel` beside `fib vpp` under `vpp` commits as `docs/guide/vpp.md` describes ("To run both, write both"), and no second VPP writer is added. `fib p4`, which declares no plane, is not judged and still stops the engine from adding a writer. Besides `fib-kernel`, only `sysctl` sets the mark (AC-5), for its own use, setting the Linux kernel's own tunables: the decision row allows these two host-side uses. With no `fib` block the engine loads the selected plane's writer as today. Thomas's "atm" leaves the one-block form open to a later decision; until then it is refused |
| AC-15 | Flow export with conntrack flow records enabled (`flow-export conntrack enabled true`) under `interface backend vpp`, sampling off (C-4 (a)) | Commit, start, reload and validate refuse it as AC-5 refuses sampling, naming the conntrack container and saying the kernel conntrack table sees none of the flows VPP forwards, with the fixes `set interface backend kernel` or turning the records off. The mechanism: flow export declares a second in-use predicate on its own registration, the conntrack records being enabled, for the kernel plane | Flow export with neither sampling nor conntrack records commits under `vpp`, and both commit under `kernel`. The worker programs nothing; it is refused because it only reads what the kernel sees, which the amended decision row names |
| AC-16 | A daemon start whose stored configuration holds IPsec (`vpn ipsec`) and `interface backend vpp`, or any other mismatch of AC-1 to AC-5, AC-8, AC-14 and AC-15 (C-5) | `ze` refuses to start, naming the mismatch and its fix with the words commit, reload and `ze config validate` give; for IPsec, `vpn ipsec` and `set interface backend kernel`. No mechanism is added: one verdict in `LoadConfig` serves start and reload, the loader is not told which it serves, and no declaration carries a start mark. This sets aside the owner decision of 2026-11-19 for IPsec under `vpp`, and `ipsec-8` is corrected to name the exception | The 2026-11-19 ruling stays for the IPsec block's own conflicts: a boot configuration with an ICMP overlap, or an SPD entry that fails to install, still starts and is reported. A stored configuration with no mismatch starts as today. `ze doctor` on the stored file reports the row with no daemon running |
| AC-17 | `connection-state`, `flow-offload` or `mark-set` in a `firewall` block under `firewall backend vpp` (I, C-2 (b)) | The three `ze:backend "nft"` statements are deleted from the firewall YANG module, the walk finds no annotation, and the VPP firewall verifier decides, one outcome per node. `connection-state`: accepted for `established`, `related` or both, programmed as `PERMIT_REFLECT`; a state set holding `new` or `invalid` is refused by `verifyConnState`. `mark-set`: accepted in a filter chain within `verifyClassifyConstraints` (no `limit` in the same term, one port rather than a range, IPv4 addresses) and refused in a NAT chain. `flow-offload`: still refused, and not by the verifier. The action must name a flowtable its table declares (`validateAction` in `internal/component/firewall/validate.go`, on every backend), and the `flowtable` list keeps its own annotation, `ze:backend "kernel"` after N-1 (`"nft"` today, `ze-firewall-conf.yang`), which is not one of the three. So the engine walk refuses the flowtable node at commit, reload, validate and start, and that annotation is the only refusal `LoadConfig` makes, since no verifier runs there. The refusal names the flowtable node, no longer `flow-offload`. `verifyTable` would also refuse the flowtable and the verifier's `FlowOffload` branch stays unreachable from operator config; neither is the refusal the operator reads. A `flow-offload` naming an undeclared flowtable is refused by `validateAction`, on `nft` as on `vpp`, as today. `flow-offload`'s own annotation is deleted, as Thomas confirmed (P-2: "p2 delete") | `firewall backend kernel` accepts the three as before. `connection-state` and `mark-set` each owe a real-VPP proof in `./le test deployment vpp` before the generated page or `fw-6-firewall-vpp.md` announces them; `flow-offload` owes none and has a refusal test. The verifier's other refusals are unchanged |
| AC-18 | A plugin named only with `--plugin` (the test uses `ze.flowspec-firewall`), and a candidate that is a mismatch only because of it (`interface backend vpp`, no `firewall backend vpp`), at each door (Q-2, C-5) | Every door refuses it with the verdict of AC-3: daemon start, reload, a pushed candidate judged before boot, a candidate pushed to the running managed daemon, the SSH editor commit, the web editor commit and the web upload, a REST or gRPC config session commit, a gNMI Set, the SSH config upload, `ze config validate`, an offline editing session, a `ze --plugin <name> config edit` session with no daemon running, and `ze doctor` reports the row. The mechanism is one record: the `ze` dispatcher records the `--plugin` list once, where it is parsed and before any verb runs; one function of the config component decides the candidate's plugin selection from the tree, that record and the command-line plugins its caller holds; `loadConfig` hands it its own `cliPlugins` parameter, `verifyTree` hands it none, and no door hands the verdict a list. No constructor, hook, root handler or build input gains a plugin parameter. `startEphemeralDaemon`, which `ze config edit` calls when no daemon runs, passes the recorded list as global `--plugin` arguments before `start`, so the ephemeral daemon records the same list and its SSH editor judges with it; with an empty record its argv is today's. The upload receipt and `startEphemeralDaemon` only display or forward the list; the selection function alone decides with it. `judgeConfigBeforeBoot` hands `LoadConfig` the `plugins` it holds. `verifyTree` hands the merged selection to `refuseUnresolvableDependencies` too, so a command-line plugin whose dependency this build lacks, or a command-line name that does not resolve, is refused at validate with the words start gives. The SSH upload keeps its fixed receipt, and its fix line carries the daemon's `--plugin` arguments, so the command it names gives the verdict the upload met | The same candidate is accepted at every door of a daemon started without the `--plugin`, and by `ze config validate` without it: then nothing selects the plugin. A process that parsed no `--plugin` records an empty list, which is the true answer and not a missing one: the setup flavor starts its daemon with none. When `ze config validate` is answered by a running Ze (a file that declares an external plugin), that daemon judges with the list it was started with, not the caller's: Known Limitations |
| AC-19 | N-1: the selector values and their defaults. A configuration that sets `interface backend netlink`, `firewall backend nft` or `traffic control backend tc`; one that writes no selector; the repository's configurations, `ze:backend` annotations, registrations and pages | The schema default of each of the three selectors is `kernel`, and `kernel` and `vpp` are the values each accepts in a build with `ze_vpp`. An old value is refused at commit, start, reload and `ze config validate`, and reported by `ze doctor`, one refusal per selector, joined: "interface backend "netlink" names no backend this build registers / the backends are kernel, vpp / `set interface backend kernel`", the fix line being the selector's path and its schema default, read from the schema. Nothing in the code names an old value: the refusal is the verdict's refusal of a selector value no registered backend answers (Data Flow step 4). Every `ze:backend` annotation names `kernel` where it named `netlink`, `nft` or `tc`, and the supporting list a walk refusal prints says `kernel`. The kernel backends register as `kernel`; `fib-kernel` declares `DataPlane: "kernel"`; the PPPoE kernel provider and the MPLS kernel reader register under `kernel`. `docs/guide/dataplane-features.md` (until AC-12 generates it) and `docs/guide/vpp.md` name the selector values `kernel` and `vpp`, and every repository user of an old value is converted (rows U-14 to U-21) | `vpp` is unchanged. Go package names (`firewall/nft`, `traffic/netlink`, `iface/netlink`) and the nftables, tc and netlink mechanisms the pages describe stay; the doctor dependency and kernel capability subsystem named `netlink` name the socket, not a plane, and stay. A configuration with no selector commits as today, on `kernel`. No alias and no conversion code exist (`ai/rules/no-layering.md`; pre-release): the refusal is the whole treatment of an old value |
| AC-20 | N-1, the population after the work: every whole-word occurrence of `netlink`, `nft` or `tc`, in any case, in every tracked file (Changed-Wire Census) | `./le doc check retired-selector-values`, a sweep beside the retired-command sweep in `internal/le/doc/check`, walks the tracked files through the same tracked-file walk (`walkTracked`) and passes over the tree: it refuses, naming the file, the line, the old value and the fix `kernel`, every occurrence that no declared exception covers, and after the work there is none. The exceptions are the class exceptions F-1 to F-4 (by path: history, the records `retiredRecords` declares, read from it and never copied, the reboot snapshots among them as the files their manifest pins, with the owned journal rows section of every other spec skipped as `retiredOwnedRowsAfter` skips it; not Ze's text; the sweep itself and this spec; the proofs of the refusal) and O-1 to O-7 (by the occurrence and the characters around it: Go code, a joined name, all capitals, the traffic YANG module prefix, the mechanism word after it, `netlink` after a mechanism word, a program's argument vector), F-1 declared once in `retiredRecords` and every other class declared once in the sweep with its structural rule and its reason, as `retiredDeclarations` and `retiredWords` are; and the line exceptions, each declared with its file, the old value, the text of the line that keeps it and its reason, as `retiredExceptions` is. No class is a list of phrases that may name a backend, so an occurrence in a shape nobody listed is refused, not missed. The three retired values and their successor `kernel` are declared once, in the sweep, since no product code names them (AC-19). It runs as a stage of the full verify, beside `retired-commands`. A golden that echoes the YANG is a tracked file and is swept, so one not taken again from its producer reddens the sweep. `TestEveryBackendAnnotationNamesARegisteredBackend` proves from the loaded schema and the registry that every `ze:backend` argument names a backend registered under the selector whose subtree it annotates; registration names, plane keys and defaults are proven from the registry by AC-11 and AC-19 | No occurrence is read by hand: one that names the kernel mechanism in a shape no class covers is reworded or declared as a line exception with its reason. The retired-command sweep reads the same records, so it skips every snapshot the reboot-assets manifest pins, where it skipped four by path, and still sweeps the README, the manifest and an unpinned file under `base/` or `unfinished/`; a manifest it cannot read refuses that sweep with 2 as it refuses this one (`TestRetiredRecordsCoverExactlyTheManifestPins`). An untracked file is not read: `docs/features.md` is generated and git ignores it, so its source `features/*.md` is swept |

## End-to-End User Stories

| # | User does | Path through system | Test proving it works |
|---|-----------|--------------------|-----------------------|
| 1 | Moves a router to VPP by setting `interface backend vpp`, leaving a kernel firewall | commit -> config doors -> verdict -> refusal with `set firewall backend vpp` | `test/parse/dataplane-vpp-refuses-kernel-firewall.ci` |
| 2 | Adds an IPsec tunnel on a VPP router | commit -> verdict -> refusal naming IPsec | `test/parse/dataplane-vpp-refuses-ipsec.ci` |
| 3 | Runs `ze doctor` on a box whose stored config holds a mismatch | doctor -> same function -> row with the fix | `test/plugin/dataplane-mismatch-doctor.ci` |
| 4 | Reads which features VPP carries | site and `docs/` page generated from the declarations | `TestDataPlanePageMatchesDeclarations` |
