| `dataplane-door-ssh-editor-sees-command-line-plugin`, `dataplane-door-web-editor-sees-command-line-plugin`, `dataplane-door-api-sees-command-line-plugin`, `dataplane-door-gnmi-sees-command-line-plugin`, `dataplane-door-upload-sees-command-line-plugin` | `test/plugin/` | Q-2, C-5 | AC-18, one file for each daemon door: a daemon started with `--plugin ze.flowspec-firewall` refuses, at that door's own validation, a commit that sets `interface backend vpp` with no `firewall backend vpp`, and the stored configuration is unchanged | |
| `dataplane-door-pushed-config-sees-command-line-plugin` | `test/managed/` | Q-2, C-5 | AC-18: a pushed candidate with the same mismatch is refused before boot when `ze` is started with the `--plugin`, and the previous configuration boots; the same candidate pushed to the running managed daemon is refused by its push handler and the running configuration stays | |
| `dataplane-door-ephemeral-editor-sees-command-line-plugin` | `test/plugin/` | Q-2, C-5 | AC-18: `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall config edit` on a `kernel` file with no daemon running starts the ephemeral daemon; the session's commit of `interface backend vpp` with no `firewall backend vpp` is refused, naming the `--plugin` argument, and the stored configuration is unchanged; the same session without the `--plugin` commits | |
| `dataplane-vpp-refuses-flow-conntrack-records` | `test/parse/` | C-4 (a) | AC-15: `conntrack enabled true` under `vpp` is refused | |
| `dataplane-vpp-ipsec-refuses-start` | `test/plugin/` | C-5 | AC-16: start is refused naming `vpn ipsec` | |
| `firewall-vpp-connection-state-accepted` | `test/parse/` | C-2 (b) | AC-17: `connection-state established,related` commits on VPP; its real-VPP proof is in `./le test deployment vpp` | |
| `firewall-vpp-connection-state-new-refused` | `test/parse/` | C-2 (b) | AC-17: `connection-state new` is refused by the verifier | |
| `firewall-vpp-mark-set-accepted` | `test/parse/` | C-2 (b) | AC-17: `mark-set` in a filter chain commits on VPP; its real-VPP proof is in `./le test deployment vpp` | |
| `firewall-vpp-flow-offload-refused` | `test/parse/` | C-2 (b) | AC-17: `flow-offload` with its declared flowtable is still refused on VPP by the `flowtable` list's `ze:backend "kernel"` annotation; the refusal names the flowtable node (`firewall table <name> flowtable <name>`) and backend `vpp` | |
| `dataplane-refuses-old-backend-names` | `test/parse/` | N-1 | AC-19: `ze config validate` on a file with `interface backend netlink`, `firewall backend nft` and `traffic control backend tc` is refused with three joined refusals, each naming its selector and the old value and ending `set interface backend kernel`, `set firewall backend kernel` and `set traffic control backend kernel`; the same file with `kernel` in each validates | |
| `dataplane-old-backend-names-refuse-start` | `test/plugin/` | N-1 | AC-19: a daemon started on the same file exits with the same words, before any plugin starts; `ze doctor` on it reports the rows under `doctor-dataplane-mismatch` | |
| `dataplane-kernel-defaults-accepted` | `test/parse/` | N-1 | AC-19: a file with `interface`, `firewall` and `traffic control` blocks that write no `backend` and that holds an interface `bridge`, a firewall `connection-mark` match and a traffic `rate` (each annotated `ze:backend "kernel"`) validates, so each default resolves to `kernel`; the same file with `interface backend vpp` and no other change is refused naming the `bridge` node | |

Each refusal test is proven by a recorded red: with the verdict, or the tree
validator it drives, disabled the test fails, because the same file then
validates.

## Files to Modify
- `internal/component/plugin/registry/registry.go` - the data-plane declaration fields on `Registration` (the plane, and in use as a tree predicate or as "the candidate selects this plugin"), the selector declaration, and the registry function a package with no registration (L2TP, GTSM) calls. The one mark of Data Flow step 6, the host-side mark ("this declaration acts on the Linux host itself, beside the selected data plane"), is added for two reasons: C-3 (a), `fib-kernel` programming the Linux route table for the router's own traffic, and Q-1 (b), `sysctl` setting the Linux kernel's own tunables. No start mark exists
- `internal/component/config/optional_provider.go`, `internal/component/config/plugin_verify.go`, `internal/component/config/loader.go` - call the verdict at both doors, handing it the candidate's plugin selection: in `loadConfig` after `refuseAbsentOptionalProviders`, with `ExtractPluginsFromTree` merged with the command-line plugins; in `verifyTree` beside `refuseUnresolvableDependencies`. Both ask the one selection function, which merges the recorded command-line plugins and the command-line plugins its caller holds: `loadConfig` hands it its `cliPlugins` parameter, `verifyTree` none; `verifyTree` hands the merged selection to `refuseUnresolvableDependencies` as well. `LoadConfig` keeps its signature, and its `cliPlugins` parameter keeps deciding what starts and which module text is joined, and the verdict reads it too, so a caller that holds a list with no record behind it (the chaos runner) is judged with it (R-9): start and reload are judged alike (C-5)

The record matching moves out of `retiredExcluded` into one function that answers, for a checkout root, whether a tracked path is covered by a record: it reads the manifest once, answers an absent manifest as pinning nothing, and returns an error, which the sweep reports as unreadable with exit 2, for a manifest it cannot read or parse, an entry with a set digest and no path, or a pinned path outside the manifest's directory. `retiredExcluded` keeps the vendored tree and `retiredDeclarations` and asks that function for the records; `sweepRetired` builds it before `walkTracked`, and the retired selector value sweep calls the same function for F-1 (AC-20). The file's header comment, which names the sweep's declared records, follows

(`test/draft/` and every `test/interop*/` scenario included), `etc/` and
`examples/`, and the Go files under `internal/` and `cmd/` that write a config
(fixtures, deployment scenarios, unit tests), for `backend vpp` in any spelling,
a `vpp` or `fib` block, and the firewall, traffic, WireGuard, IPsec, L2TP,
sampling, copp, flowspec-firewall and `ttl` users; and for the symbols this spec
removes (`validateBackendGate`, `ValidateBackendFeaturesJSON`,
`runBackendGate`, `checkBackends`, `doctor-vrrp-backend-unusable`). No interop
scenario, no file under `etc/` or `examples/` and no draft selects VPP. Every
conversion that changes a test's input, expected output or fixture is judged by
`ze-test-judge` before it lands (R-1).

## Files to Create
- `internal/component/config/dataplane_verdict.go` - the verdict and its doctor check
- `internal/plugins/iface/vpp/verify_wireguard.go` - the function that judges a WireGuard interface under `vpp` against `vpp plugins wireguard` over a whole config tree; the `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` tree validator and the `vpp-wireguard-plugin` doctor check both call it
- `internal/component/firewall/producer_verify.go` - the config table builder registry and the `firewall-producer-tables` tree validator: resolve the candidate's firewall backend (its leaf or the schema default), run every registered builder over the candidate tree and that backend's verifier over the result, joining the refusals (AC-8)
- `internal/le/doc/check/retired_selector.go` - the retired selector value sweep of AC-20: the three retired values and their successor `kernel`, declared once; the occurrence rule (every whole-word occurrence, in any case); the class exceptions F-2 to F-4 and O-1 to O-7 of the Changed-Wire Census, each with its structural rule and its reason; F-1, which it does not declare: it asks `retired.go` for the records `retiredRecords` declares, through the function the retired-command sweep skips its records with, and skips the owned journal rows section through `retiredOwnedRowsAfter` itself, so it holds no copy of either; and the line exceptions, each with its file, old value, line text and reason. Its fixtures live under `internal/le/doc/check/testdata/retired_selector/`. It walks the tracked files through `walkTracked` (`repository.go`), the walk the links and retired-command sweeps share. The documentation drift gate (`internal/le/doc/yangcontract/drift.go`) was read for this and is not the home: each of its checks compares one named document with a registry or a count, none walks the tracked tree, and it holds no history records or declared exceptions, which the retired-command sweep already has
- The unit test files and `.ci` files of the Test Plan

The four "explicit `fib` block wins" passages stand unchanged (P-1 (b)); `docs/architecture/firewall/table-ownership-and-shutdown-flush.md`, the producer census and the `firewall-producer-tables` validator; `docs/research/l2tpv2-ze-integration.md`, 11.3; the departed pages, each departure presented for Thomas's approval with the design (Files to Modify) |

Discovery rows: `ai/INDEX.md` gains the verdict, `doctor-dataplane-mismatch`, the producer-table validator and the generated page by hand; `ai/CODE-TO-DOCS.md` and `ai/DOCS-TO-CODE.md` are regenerated by `./le doc index update` |

## Design presentation

### What changes

Each backend and each feature that writes forwarding state says, in its own
package, which data plane it programs. When a configuration puts a feature on a
data plane other than the one `interface backend` selects, Ze refuses it at
commit, at start and in `ze config validate`, and `ze doctor` reports it, with
the line that fixes it. The feature-by-data-plane page is generated from the
same declarations.

The selectors name the plane they program: `interface backend`,
`firewall backend` and `traffic control backend` each take `kernel` or `vpp`
and default to `kernel` (N-1). A configuration that still says `netlink`, `nft`
or `tc` is refused with `set <selector> kernel`; nothing accepts the old names.

### Before and after

| # | Configuration | Before | After |
|---|---------------|--------|-------|
| 1 | `interface backend vpp`, a `firewall` block, no `firewall backend` | Commits. Rules go to kernel nftables. | Refused: "firewall uses backend "kernel", which programs the Linux kernel, and interface backend is "vpp" / VPP forwards the packets, so these rules would filter nothing it forwards / `set firewall backend vpp`" |
| 2 | `interface backend vpp` with `vpn ipsec` | Commits. Kernel XFRM is programmed. | Refused at commit, start, reload and validate: "vpn ipsec cannot run with interface backend "vpp" / IPsec programs kernel XFRM only, and VPP forwards the packets, so the tunnels would carry no traffic / `set interface backend kernel`". A box that stores this configuration no longer boots until it is fixed |
| 3 | `fib vpp` with `interface backend kernel` | Commits. Each route with an interface next hop fails at install. | Refused: "fib vpp programs VPP, and interface backend is "kernel" / its routes name interfaces VPP does not have / `set interface backend vpp`" |
| 4 | WireGuard interface on `vpp`, plugin leaf off, or a later commit that only turns the leaf off | Commits. `ze doctor` reports an error. Apply fails. | Refused at commit, validate, start and reload with `set vpp plugins wireguard true`; doctor says the same |
| 5 | `snat` with a source address match on `firewall backend vpp` | Commits. The match is ignored. | Refused, naming the term |
| 6 | `control-plane-protection`, or a `firewall irr interface` binding, with `firewall backend vpp` | Commits. The table is refused when copp or firewall-irr applies it; at start that stops the startup tier. | Refused at commit, validate, start and reload with the VPP verifier's message, before any plugin starts |
| 7 | `interface backend kernel`, kernel firewall, IPsec, L2TP | Commits | Commits, unchanged |
| 8 | `interface backend vpp`, `firewall backend vpp`, `traffic control backend vpp`, `fib vpp` | Commits | Commits, unchanged |
| 9 | `interface backend vpp` with `plugin internal flowspec-firewall { use flowspec-firewall; }` and no `firewall backend vpp` | Commits. FlowSpec rules are written to kernel nftables through the OS default backend. | Refused, naming the `plugin` block, saying its rules program the Linux kernel and VPP forwards the packets, with `set firewall backend vpp` or `delete` of the block as the fix |
| 10 | `interface backend vpp` with a BGP peer that sets `ttl max 1` | Commits. Kernel host routes and a kernel nft table are programmed; VPP forwards the BGP packets with neither, so GTSM protects nothing | Refused, naming the peer's `ttl` leaf, saying GTSM programs the Linux kernel only, with `set interface backend kernel` or `delete` of the `ttl` leaf as the fix. RFC 5082 GTSM is unavailable on a VPP router until a VPP implementation exists |
| 11 | `interface backend vpp` with `fib kernel` and no `fib vpp` block, and a static route | Commits. No VPP writer is loaded, so VPP's FIB gets no route | Refused at commit, validate, start and reload, for now: "fib kernel programs the Linux route table only, and interface backend is "vpp" / VPP forwards the packets, and with no fib vpp block its FIB gets no route / `set fib vpp`". With `fib vpp` beside it, it commits as today |
| 11a | `interface backend vpp` with a `sysctl` block, an `rp_filter` key included | Commits | Commits, unchanged. Every key is applied to the Linux kernel as on any data plane, so a setting about kernel behaviour works unchanged; `rp_filter`, a key about packet forwarding, acts only on the packets that reach Linux, not on the packets VPP forwards. The generated page and the guide say so |
| 11b | `ze --plugin ze.flowspec-firewall config validate site.conf`, the file setting `interface backend vpp` and no `firewall backend vpp` | Valid: validate drops the `--plugin`. A daemon started with the same `--plugin` boots and writes FlowSpec rules to kernel nftables | Refused at validate and at start with the same words, naming the `--plugin` argument, with `set firewall backend vpp` as the fix |
| 12 | `interface backend vpp` with `flow-export conntrack enabled true` | Commits. The records hold none of the flows VPP forwards | Refused, naming the conntrack container, saying the kernel conntrack table sees none of the flows VPP forwards, with `set interface backend kernel` or turning the records off as the fix |
| 13 | `firewall backend vpp` with a term matching `connection-state established,related`, or one that writes `mark-set` | Refused at commit by the `ze:backend "nft"` annotation | Commits and is programmed on VPP. `connection-state new` is refused by the verifier, saying VPP models established and related only |
| 14 | `firewall backend vpp` with a `flow-offload` term and its flowtable | Refused at commit by the `ze:backend "nft"` annotations on `flow-offload` and on the `flowtable` list | Still refused at commit, validate, reload and start, now naming the flowtable only: the `flowtable` list keeps its annotation, `ze:backend "kernel"` after N-1, because VPP has no flowtable |
| 15 | `interface { backend netlink; }`, `firewall { backend nft; }` or `traffic control { backend tc; }` | Commits: these are today's names, and the defaults | Refused at commit, validate, start and reload, one refusal per selector: "interface backend "netlink" names no backend this build registers / the backends are kernel, vpp / `set interface backend kernel`" |
| 16 | `interface`, `firewall` and `traffic control` blocks that write no `backend` | Commits on `netlink`, `nft` and `tc` | Commits on `kernel` for all three; a refusal's supporting list reads `(supported: kernel)` where it read `(supported: netlink)` |

Each quoted refusal is three lines: problem, why, fix. The slash marks the line
break.

### Alternatives set aside

| Alternative | Why set aside |
|-------------|---------------|
| Let a kernel-only feature declare a host-side meaning (it acts on traffic punted to Linux) and show that scope through the implied-configuration display | Thomas set it aside on 2026-11-23: "the commit should fail". It would keep kernel protection of a VPP box's own control plane, at the cost of a second accepted meaning for the same configuration |
| YANG `must` statements between modules | A module would name another module's path, so the firewall schema would name interfaces and the IPsec schema would name a backend it does not own |
| More doctor checks only | Reports after the fact; the commit still accepts the configuration |
| A central feature-by-data-plane table in the config component | A central enumeration: a new feature would be accepted until someone edits it |
| One hand check per plugin, as VRRP and the container plugin do today, or one original-tree validator per feature as PPPoE does | Already drifting: one repeats its annotation, the other hardcodes two schema defaults; and the tree validator loop reports only the first refusal |
| Reuse `ConfigNeed` unchanged with a pseudo-provider per data plane | It answers "is the plugin in the build"; bending it would give one declaration two meanings |
| C-2 (a): keep the three `ze:backend "nft"` annotations, so operator config stays refused on VPP | Thomas chose (b), 2026-11-23. It kept two declarations of one fact, the annotation and the verifier, disagreeing |
| C-3 (b): refuse `fib kernel` under `interface backend vpp` | Thomas chose (a), 2026-11-23. It ended the documented deployment in which Linux keeps routes for its own services beside VPP |
| C-4 (b): accept conntrack flow records under `vpp` | Thomas chose (a), 2026-11-23. The export would be silently empty for forwarded traffic |
| C-5, first answer: start without the mismatching part, report it, and refuse every commit until it is fixed | Thomas replaced it the same day: "You know what. Refuse invalid configuration at boot", "simpler". It needed a start entry of the loader, a rule for which part each refusal withholds, and a running configuration that differs from the stored one |
| C-5 (b): a per-feature mark, set by the IKE engine, that reports an IPsec mismatch at start and refuses it elsewhere | Set aside with the same answer: one verdict, the same at every door, with no start mark and no input telling the loader that a load is a start |
| Q-1 (a): refuse `sysctl` under `interface backend vpp` as a kernel-only feature | Thomas chose (b), 2026-11-23: "so we can consider mix setup when we add vrf". It would have refused every VPP configuration that tunes the Linux kernel, settings about kernel behaviour included, which work unchanged on a VPP box |
| Q-2, the earlier reading: keep `ze config validate` blind to `--plugin` and record the gap | Thomas answered "it should start the plugin to get the yang": validate sees what start sees. A validate that answers valid for a file the daemon then refuses breaks the hub door's promise |
| Thread the command-line plugins as a parameter to every door: `cmdValidate` through the `config` root handler, `Run`, `RunWithStorage` and the `subcommandHandlers` type; `ValidateContent` and its six callers; `NewConfigValidator` and its five; `configValidationHook` through `apiBuildInputs` and `buildGNMISessionManager`; the upload runtime through `sessionMutations.bind`; the web editor factory | It has no process-wide state, and each call site shows what it judges with. It is set aside because the fact is one per process and the doors are twelve: every door built later must be remembered, and a door handed a list can be handed `nil`, which is how `judgeConfigBeforeBoot` and `ze doctor` already judge without the plugins today though both sit beside them. The offline sessions share `ValidateContent` with the daemon doors, so each would need a list it has no reason to hold. The record has the hazards of shared state, and they are bounded: it is written once, before any reader, under the mutex `RecordStartedPlugins` already uses; the judging function takes the selection as an argument, so only three tests write the record, each restoring it (R-10); and an unwritten record means "no command-line plugin", which is true of every process that parsed none (R-9) |
| Two writers of the record: `runYANGConfig` beside `RecordStartedPlugins`, and the `config` root handler from `registry.RuntimeContext.Plugins` | Each writer sits where its process kind is assembled. Set aside for one writer at the place `--plugin` is parsed: a second writer is a second place to forget, `ze doctor` and the offline sessions would need a third and a fourth, and the daemon's list and the dispatcher's are the same slice |
| P-1 (a): with `fib kernel` and no `fib vpp` block under `vpp`, load `fib-vpp` as implied configuration | Thomas chose (b), "atm". It changed `fibPluginChosen`, four documents that say an explicit `fib` block always wins, and needed the implied-configuration display of `spec-bus-2-implied-display-and-grant-refusals` |
| N-1, keep the implementation names (`netlink`, `nft`, `tc`) as selector values and give each backend a separate plane declaration | Thomas chose the plane names: "so we have vpp and kernel?", "please do it". Keeping them needed a plane argument on every registration beside a name that says something else, and an operator had to learn three words for one kernel |
| N-1, accept the old values as aliases of `kernel`, or convert a stored configuration that names one | Ze is pre-release: `ai/rules/no-layering.md` forbids keeping both, and `ai/META-DECISIONS.md`, "Ze is pre-release", writes the code that converts one format change only once a stable release exists. Every repository configuration is converted in the same work; an operator's is refused with the fix |
| N-1, keep a plane argument on `RegisterBackend` beside the renamed name | The name now is the plane, so the argument would declare one fact twice (`ai/rules/principles.md`). When a second implementation of one plane exists (the eBPF cost the decision row accepts), its own value and its plane declaration are designed then |
| P-2, keep: keep `flow-offload`'s own `ze:backend "nft"` annotation | Thomas confirmed the deletion, "p2 delete": the `flowtable` list's annotation alone states that VPP has no flowtable |

### Documents departed from

`docs/architecture/config/deprecated-options.md`, "Compatibility rules", row
"Older pre-release Ze syntax": "The parse fails, naming the line and the
unknown keyword. Edit the file to the current syntax." Before this work, that
is the only outcome the page gives an older config. After it, a config that
names a selector value N-1 retired still parses, because the three `backend`
leaves are strings, and the data-plane verdict refuses it at `ze config
validate`, commit, start and reload. What the operator sees for
`interface { backend netlink; }`, its three lines joined by slashes: "interface
backend "netlink" names no backend this build registers / the backends are
kernel, vpp / `set interface backend kernel`". The row is corrected to name both outcomes: an unknown keyword fails the
parse, and a renamed value of a string leaf parses and is refused by the
commit check that judges it, naming the leaf and the fix line. The section's
rule above the table, that no conversion is written before a stable release
and the repository's configs are converted in the same change, is unchanged.
The alternative, a parse-time refusal through a YANG `enumeration` type on
the three leaves, is set aside: the values are the names the backends
register, which the build decides (`ze_vpp`), and an enumeration would be a
second declaration of the registry (AC-11).

`docs/architecture/core-design.md`, section "14a. Commit-Time Backend Capability
Gate", row Wiring: "iface, firewall, and traffic call the gate from
`OnConfigure` (startup) and from the parse their `OnConfigVerify` (reload) and
their `InProcessConfigVerifier` share." After this work the row says that the
config component runs one walk over the whole config tree at its two doors
(`LoadConfig` and `verifyTree`), for every declared
selector, and that no component calls the gate. The operator sees the same
refusal text for the same node.

`docs/architecture/core-design.md`, section "14b. Firewall and Traffic
Control", the traffic reactor paragraph: "The backend feature gate
(`config.ValidateBackendFeaturesJSON`) runs in both `OnConfigure` and
`OnConfigVerify`, so tc-only feature annotations land as a one-line
declaration once `spec-fw-7-traffic-vpp` introduces a second backend. Firewall
will follow the same pattern in `spec-fw-8`." Before: the traffic component
calls the gate itself from both callbacks, with its own root and its own
backend. After: the traffic component calls no gate (AC-10); the config
component's one walk judges the `traffic control` annotations against the
`traffic control backend` selector the component declares, at `LoadConfig` and
`verifyTree`. A kernel-only annotation (`ze:backend "kernel"`, N-1) is still a one-line YANG declaration. The
operator sees the same refusal for the same node. The traffic verifier's
refusals keep their words, but a configuration now reaches the verifier only
once its selectors agree: `test/traffic/traffic-vpp-reject-hfsc.ci` commits a
candidate that selects `traffic control backend vpp` and writes no `interface`
block, which is case D, so AC-4 refuses it in `LoadConfig` before the reload
transaction reaches the verifier, and its expectation `qdisc hfsc: not supported
by backend vpp` would go red. Its candidate gains `interface { backend vpp; }`
(row U-1 of Repository users the refusals reach), and every assertion it makes
stays. The paragraph is rewritten in the same work (Files to Modify).

`ai/doors/forwarding.md`, Boundaries, "Exact or refuse, at verify": "Feature
support is declared with `ze:backend` in YANG and checked by
`config.validateBackendFeatures` from `OnConfigure` and `OnConfigVerify`".
After: the same annotation is judged by one walk the config component runs at
its two doors. The operator sees no difference.

`docs/architecture/container/network.md`, "The rules": "V-19 runs the
commit-time backend gate (`config.ValidateBackendFeaturesJSON`) over the
container section with the interface backend the `interface` root selects".
After: the engine walk judges the container network list against the
data-plane selector, and the container plugin keeps only its masquerade and
published-port rules. Before and after, a container network under
`interface backend vpp` is refused naming the network path and
`set interface backend kernel`.

`docs/architecture/vrrp/vrrp-first-hop-redundancy.md`, "VPP-backed trees fail
closed": "Two mechanisms enforce it: a plugin verify check that reads
`interface.backend`, and a `ze:backend "netlink"` annotation on the vrrp
containers that iface's generic gate enforces. The schema-level gate survives a
regression in the plugin check." After: one mechanism, the annotation, judged
by the engine walk; the second line of defence is gone. Before and after, a
VRRP group under `vpp` is refused at verify; after, only the walk's path
refusal remains to refuse it.

`docs/architecture/firewall/fw-6-firewall-vpp.md`, "What VPP programs,
everything else rejected at commit": "Both apply to all traffic, so a term
carrying either may match on source address only, which is accepted and does
not narrow anything." Before: `snat` with `source-address 10.0.0.0/24` on
`firewall backend vpp` commits and translates every inside source. After: the
commit is refused naming table, chain and term, saying VPP NAT44 applies to
the whole inside interface, with the fix to delete the match or use
`firewall backend kernel`.

The same section, "`set-mark` and `limit`, in a filter chain, through a VPP
classify table", listed under "Two things beyond the ACL plugin are accepted
and programmed", and "`MatchConnState` maps to `PERMIT_REFLECT` for
established and related only." Before: a `firewall` term under
`firewall backend vpp` that writes `mark-set` or names `connection-state` is
refused at commit by its `ze:backend "nft"` annotation, as the page does not
say; the verifier branches it describes serve tables other plugins build, such
as copp's established-and-related term. After (C-2 (b), AC-17): the term
commits on VPP and is programmed through the classify table or as
`PERMIT_REFLECT`, and the page stands. These two rows now read `agrees`; the
paragraph stays because the page is silent on `flow-offload`, which stays
refused through the flowtable it must name, and gains that sentence.

`docs/architecture/traffic/cp-survival-2-copp-port179.md`, "A system plugin,
not a firewall extension": "It mirrors the `policyroute` pattern,
`RegisterTables` plus `ApplyAll`, rather than adding a firewall API." After:
copp still registers its table through `RegisterTables` and `ApplyAll` at
apply, and it also registers `translatePolicy` as a config table builder with
the firewall component from `init()`. The firewall's `firewall-producer-tables`
tree validator runs every registered builder over the candidate tree, under the
candidate's firewall backend, and runs that backend's verifier over the result
at commit, at `ze config validate`, and in `LoadConfig` at start and reload.
Before, a copp configuration under `firewall backend vpp` commits and its table
is refused when applied, which at start stops the startup tier; after, the
commit, the validate and the start are refused with the same verifier message,
before any plugin starts. The alternative that keeps the page true, copp's own
config verifier calling the existing `firewall.RunVerifier`, adds no API, but
an in-process verifier does not run at daemon start
(`docs/architecture/config/yang-config-design.md`, "Layer 3"), so at start H
would still reach `ApplyAll`; the IRR interface bindings (K) would need the
same call in a second plugin.

`docs/architecture/ike/ipsec-8-ikev2-child-xfrm.md`, "ICMP error messages
carried by a Child SA": "Owner decision, 2026-11-19: start-up never refuses the
IPsec block. A boot configuration that holds a conflict a commit would refuse
is kept". The verdict runs in `LoadConfig`, which daemon start calls. Before:
a box whose stored configuration holds IPsec and `interface backend vpp` boots,
its tunnels carry nothing VPP forwards, and nothing reports it. After: `ze`
refuses to start, naming `vpn ipsec` and `set interface backend kernel`, and
the box has no daemon until the stored configuration is fixed; `ze doctor` and
`ze config validate` give the same answer on the stored file. The ruling stays
for the IPsec block's own conflicts. Thomas confirmed this departure on
2026-11-23 (voice-transcribed): "You know what. Refuse invalid configuration at
boot", then "simpler". The alternative that keeps the page, a start that
reports the mismatch and keeps running, was his first answer to C-5 and he
replaced it. The page is corrected in the same work (Files to Modify).

The departures of N-1, each the rename Thomas decided ("please do it"), each
corrected in the same work:

`docs/architecture/core-design.md`, section "14. Interface Management", row
Backend selection: "YANG `backend` leaf (default: `netlink`).
`RegisterBackend`/`LoadBackend` in `backend.go`". Before: an operator who
writes `interface { backend netlink; }` gets the kernel backend, and so does
one who writes nothing. After: the second gets `kernel`; the first is refused
with "interface backend "netlink" names no backend this build registers / the
backends are kernel, vpp / `set interface backend kernel`". The row says
"default: `kernel`". The alternative that keeps the page, keeping `netlink`,
is the one Thomas set aside.

`docs/features/interfaces.md`, "Key Design Decisions": "`backend` leaf selects
the backend (default: `netlink`)". Before and after as above; the bullet says
`kernel`, and its own "Pure netlink" bullet, which names the mechanism, stays.

`docs/architecture/core-design.md`, section "14a. Commit-Time Backend
Capability Gate", row No backend on this host: "(netlink, nft and tc are the
Linux defaults)", and row Initial coverage, "annotated `ze:backend "netlink"`"
and the firewall's `ze:backend "nft"` annotations. Before: a refusal on a VPP
box reads `/interface/bridge: feature not supported by backend "vpp"
(supported: netlink)`. After: it reads `(supported: kernel)`, and `kernel` is
the Linux default of all three selectors.

`docs/architecture/core-design.md`, Design decisions, the third row: "the
`flowtable` list keeps its `ze:backend "nft"` annotation". Before: the row
names `nft`. After: "the `flowtable` list keeps its `ze:backend "kernel"`
annotation"; what the row decides (the verifier decides `connection-state` and
`mark-set`, `flow-offload` stays refused through the flowtable it names) is
unchanged, and so is what the operator sees: `flow-offload` refused on VPP at
the flowtable node.

`docs/architecture/plugin/plugin-system.md`, "Registration fields", row
`DataPlane`: "spelled the way `interface { backend }` names it: `netlink`,
`vpp`". Before: `fib-kernel` declares `netlink`. After: it declares `kernel`,
which is still the way `interface { backend }` names the kernel. Nothing an
operator writes changes beyond the selector value.

### Choices for Thomas

| # | Choice | Options | Decision |
|---|--------|---------|----------|
| C-1 | A kernel-only feature (kernel firewall, kernel traffic control, IPsec, L2TP, flow export sampling, policy routing) with `interface backend vpp` | (a) refuse at commit; forecloses kernel filtering of a VPP box's own punted traffic. (b) let the feature declare a host-side meaning shown by the implied-configuration display; forecloses a single rule and keeps two meanings | (a). Thomas, 2026-11-23, voice-transcribed: "should Ze refuse a kernel-only feature when the interfaces run on VPP: the commit should fail" |
| C-2 | `connection-state`, `flow-offload` and `mark-set` are `ze:backend "nft"` in YANG, and the VPP verifier has code that accepts them (I) | (a) keep the annotations: operator config stays refused on VPP, the verifier branches serve only tables built by other plugins, and the page says "Refused"; forecloses stateful rules on VPP for now. (b) remove the three annotations so the verifier decides: enables established and related state, flow offload and set-mark on VPP; owes a real-VPP proof of each before it is announced. Mechanism of (a): none; the three annotations stay and the engine walk refuses the nodes as the firewall gate does today. Mechanism of (b): the three `ze:backend "nft"` statements are deleted from the firewall YANG module, so the walk finds nothing and the VPP verifier decides. The design page follows the choice: `docs/architecture/firewall/fw-6-firewall-vpp.md` says `set-mark` is "accepted and programmed" on VPP and that `MatchConnState` "maps to `PERMIT_REFLECT`", which holds today only for tables other plugins build; under (a) those passages would have been corrected; under (b) they become true for operator config. Outcome and tests: AC-17. The author recommended (a) in this spec, since a guard should not widen what is accepted, with (b) as a feature spec; Thomas chose (b) | (b). Thomas, 2026-11-23, voice-transcribed: "C-2 b". Reading taken: the three annotations are deleted and the VPP verifier decides. Per node (read in `verify.go`): `connection-state` is accepted for established and related; `mark-set` is accepted in a filter chain within the classify limits; `flow-offload` stays refused, because it must name a declared flowtable (`validateAction`) and the `flowtable` list keeps its `ze:backend "nft"` annotation, which the engine walk judges at every door. The first two owe a real-VPP proof (AC-17). The `flow-offload` annotation is deleted with the other two, as Thomas confirmed in P-2 below ("p2 delete") |
| C-3 | A `fib kernel` block with `interface backend vpp` | (a) keep it accepted: the operator wrote the block, and `docs/guide/vpp.md` describes kernel routes beside VPP for local services ("To run both, write both `fib kernel` and `fib vpp` blocks"); forecloses nothing. Mechanism of (a): `fib-kernel` sets, in its own registration, the host-side mark, saying its declaration acts on the Linux host itself, beside the selected data plane (for `fib-kernel`, programming the Linux route table for the router's own traffic); the verdict reads the mark and reports no mismatch for it, and no engine or config code names a FIB plugin. (a) needed the decision row amended first, since the mark is the host-side declaration its forbids clause named; Thomas amended it on 2026-11-23. (a) also left one case: `fib kernel` with no `fib vpp` block under `vpp` loads no VPP writer today (`fibPluginChosen`), so VPP forwards with an empty FIB; AC-14 refuses that case, for now, with the fix that adds `fib vpp` (P-1 (b) below). (b) refuse it under C-1 as a kernel-only feature; forecloses the documented two-block deployment and needs a statement of how Linux reaches its own routes on a VPP box. Under (b), `test/plugin/fib-vpp-coexist-with-fib-kernel.ci`, which proves that coexistence (spec-vpp-0-umbrella AC-10), would have become the start refusal of the configuration it boots, and `test/parse/fib-vpp-config-valid.ci` would have lost its `kernel { }` block; under (a), chosen, both only gain `interface { backend vpp; }` (rows U-7 and U-8). Mechanism of (b): none added; `fib-kernel` already declares `DataPlane: "netlink"`, and the general rule refuses a declared plane other than the selected one when its block is written. Outcome and tests: AC-14. The author recommended (b): the decision row as Thomas first set it on 2026-11-23 (`docs/architecture/core-design.md`, Design decisions) refused a feature that programs only the Linux kernel while the interfaces run on VPP, and forbade "letting a feature declare a host-side (LCP-punted) meaning in order to stay accepted"; (a) rests on exactly that host-side meaning (kernel routes for local services), so the row implied (b) until Thomas amended it. Evidence still missing for the chosen (a): nothing read proves the two writers work together, and VPP's `linux_nl` plugin may already copy kernel routes into VPP | (a). Thomas, 2026-11-23, voice-transcribed: "C-3 a". Reading taken: `fib kernel` stays accepted beside `interface backend vpp`, through a mark `fib-kernel` sets on its own registration, and the decision row is amended to allow this one host-side use. The sub-case he was not asked about, `fib kernel` with no `fib vpp` block, he answered in P-1 below: (b), "atm", so it is refused for now and `fib kernel` beside `fib vpp` stays accepted (AC-14) |

Mechanism of (a): flow export declares, on its own registration, a second in-use predicate (`conntrack enabled` is `true`) for the kernel plane, judged like sampling. Mechanism of (b): flow export declares no plane for the conntrack worker, and the guide says the records cover only what the kernel sees. Outcome and tests: AC-15. The IKE engine sets, on its own plane declaration, the mark "a mismatch found at daemon start is reported, not refused"; the verdict reads the mark, and neither `LoadConfig` nor any config code names IPsec. Then, superseding it: "You know what. Refuse invalid configuration at boot", and his reason: "simpler". | Q-1 | `sysctl` under `interface backend vpp`. Its keys set the Linux kernel's own tunables, applied to the kernel exactly as on any data plane: a setting about kernel behaviour works unchanged on a VPP box, and only a key about packet forwarding acts on the packets that reach Linux rather than the packets VPP forwards (an `rp_filter` key, A-1). The first decision row allowed only `fib kernel` as a host-side use | (a) refuse it under C-1 as a kernel-only feature; forecloses tuning the Linux kernel of a VPP box, kernel behaviour included. (b) keep all of `sysctl` accepted as a second host-side use, declared by `sysctl` with the host-side mark, and document that a key about packet forwarding acts only on the packets that reach Linux; needs the decision row amended to name it | (b). Thomas: "q1 b - so we can consider mix setup when we add vrf". Reading taken: every `sysctl` key stays accepted under `vpp`, the reason being mixed setups once VRFs are added; the first decision row now names both host-side uses, and the `rp_filter` consequence is documented, not refused (AC-5, Known Limitations). Thomas later clarified, voice-transcribed: "They should still affect the settings on the system sysctl. Not all settings are about interfaces or routing; they can be about kernel behavior that we want to modify." Reading taken: the design is unchanged; `sysctl` is applied to the Linux kernel exactly as on any data plane, so a setting about kernel behaviour works unchanged on a VPP box, and only a key about packet forwarding (`rp_filter`, `forwarding`) acts on the packets that reach Linux rather than the packets VPP forwards. The spec's wording, which read as if `sysctl` reached nothing on a VPP box, was corrected to say so |
| Q-2 | A plugin named only on the daemon command line (`--plugin`) was seen by `LoadConfig` at start and not by `ze config validate`, which reads no plugin list, so a file could validate and then not boot (A-10); the second decision row forbids a verdict that differs between doors | (a) give `ze config validate` the `--plugin` arguments the daemon takes, and obtain each named plugin's declarations as the daemon would. (b) accept the command-line plugin at start too, keeping a configuration that filters nothing VPP forwards. (c) record the gap | (a). Thomas: "q2 I replied elsewhere another agent should have written it down, it should start the plugin to get the yang". His earlier ruling is in `plan/release/fixes/spec-explicit-plugin-and-attach-config.md` (A-Q2, P-1, AC-8, AC-14): with no daemon, a command takes an external program's declaration in query mode. Reading taken: `ze config validate` reads the same `--plugin` arguments and merges them as `LoadConfig` does, a compiled-in plugin's declarations coming from the registry and an external program's as that spec defines, so the one verdict sees the same plugin set at validate and at start. This spec owns the validate argument; that spec owns query mode (Depends, AC-3) |
| P-1 | `fib kernel` with no `fib vpp` block under `interface backend vpp`, the sub-case C-3 did not ask about. Today it loads no VPP writer (`fibPluginChosen`), so VPP forwards with an empty FIB | (a) load `fib-vpp` as implied configuration, shown by the implied-configuration display; departs from four documents that say an explicit `fib { }` block always wins. (b) refuse it with the fix `set fib vpp`; `fibPluginChosen` and the four documents stay, and the first decision row narrows its host-side use to the block beside `fib vpp` | (b), "atm". Reading taken: refused for now at every door (AC-14), open to a later decision; `fib kernel` beside `fib vpp` stays accepted (C-3 (a)). The first decision row records "for now" |
| P-2 | `flow-offload`'s own `ze:backend "nft"` annotation under C-2 (b). Deleting it enables nothing: `flow-offload` must name a declared flowtable (`validateAction`), and the `flowtable` list keeps its annotation, so the walk refuses every `flow-offload` configuration on VPP at the flowtable node, at every door | (a) delete it with the other two, so the flowtable list alone states that VPP has no flowtable. ## Implementation Steps

1. **Phase: Wiring (MANDATORY FIRST)** -- first the plane names of N-1, in one commit with every user, since a partial rename does not boot; then the declaration fields, an empty verdict called from both config doors, the doctor code
   - Plane names (N-1, AC-19): the YANG defaults, descriptions, `ze:help` texts and `ze:backend` values, the backend and verifier registration names, `fib-kernel`'s `DataPlane`, the PPPoE provider and MPLS reader keys, the Go users and comments, and rows U-14 to U-21 converted, each test change judged by `ze-test-judge`. Tests: `TestSelectorDefaultsAreKernel`, `TestEveryPlaneNameIsAnInterfaceBackend`; the old-value refusal (`TestDataPlaneVerdictRefusesOldSelectorValues`, `dataplane-refuses-old-backend-names`, `dataplane-old-backend-names-refuse-start`) lands with the verdict's selector resolution in phase 2, and until then an old value is refused by `LoadBackend`'s "unknown backend" at apply
   - Tests: the first four Wiring Test rows (the kernel firewall file at validate, start, reload and doctor), written to fail
   - Files: the registry, the new verdict file, the optional provider, plugin verify and loader files
   - Verify: the verdict is reached from validate, start, reload and doctor (A-3); the tests fail because nothing declares yet. No mark of Data Flow step 6 is written in this phase
2. **Phase: Backend planes and selectors** -- the verdict reads each component's registered backend names as planes and refuses a selector value none answers (AC-19); components declare selectors; `ConfigDataPlane` reads the declaration; table producers declare how they are in use
   - Tests: `TestDataPlaneVerdictRefusesOldSelectorValues`, `dataplane-refuses-old-backend-names`, `dataplane-old-backend-names-refuse-start`, `dataplane-kernel-defaults-accepted`, `TestDataPlaneVerdictJudgesSelectedPluginWithoutConfigRoot`, `TestValidateReadsCommandLinePlugins`, the AC-3 and AC-4 `.ci` (the two flowspec-firewall files and `dataplane-validate-sees-command-line-plugin` included)
   - Files: the three component backend files, the six backend registrations, the three component registrations, the four table producer registrations (copp, ddos-local and anomaly shape by config root, flowspec-firewall by plugin selection), the process record and its one writer (`declared_schemas.go`, `ze_core_dispatch.go`), the selection function, `pushed_config.go`, the upload receipt, the ephemeral daemon's argv in `cmd_edit.go` and the `cmd_validate.go` usage page (Q-2, AC-18), with `TestCandidateSelectionMergesRecordedCommandLinePlugins`, `TestLoadConfigVerdictReadsItsCommandLinePlugins`, `TestVerifyTreeRefusesCommandLinePluginWithMissingDependency`, `TestDispatcherRecordsTheCommandLinePlugins`, `TestJudgeConfigBeforeBootHandsLoadConfigThePlugins`, `TestUploadReceiptNamesTheCommandLinePlugins`, `TestEphemeralDaemonArgvCarriesCommandLinePlugins` and the seven door `.ci` files, each written after A-16 is read for its door. A-17 is validated: the door parse joins no module text
   - Verify: tests fail, implement, pass; convert the users AC-3 and AC-4 reach, rows U-1 to U-4 and the traffic and firewall half of U-9, each judged by `ze-test-judge` (R-1)
3. **Phase: Engine-owned walk** -- delete the four gate call sites first, then drive the walker from the declared selectors (`ai/rules/no-layering.md`)
   - Tests: `TestBackendWalkJudgesNodeOutsideFamilyRoot`; every existing `ze:backend` `.ci` unchanged
   - Files: the gate file, and the iface, firewall, traffic, container and vrrp call sites
   - Verify: A-7; rows U-12 and U-13 converted in the same phase, each judged by `ze-test-judge`
4. **Phase: Feature planes** -- IPsec, L2TP, flow export sampling, policy routing, GTSM, the FIB writers
   - Tests: the AC-1 and AC-5 `.ci` (`dataplane-vpp-refuses-gtsm` and `dataplane-vpp-accepts-bgp-without-ttl` included), `TestDataPlaneVerdictRefusesForeignPlaneFeature`, `TestGTSMInUseFollowsTTLLeaves`

   - Verify: the producer of each declaration re-read before it is written (A-2); rows U-7, U-8, U-10, U-11 and the FIB half of U-9 converted or re-validated, U-7 and U-8 by gaining the interface line. Thomas has answered C-3, C-4 and C-5, so the phase waits on nothing: AC-14 (the `fib-kernel` mark and the rule that refuses a marked FIB writer alone, `TestDataPlaneVerdictMarks`, `TestDataPlaneVerdictRefusesMarkedFIBWriterAlone`, with `fibPluginChosen` left as it is), the `sysctl` declaration and mark of AC-5 (Q-1 (b), `dataplane-vpp-accepts-sysctl`), AC-15 (the conntrack predicate) and AC-16 (`TestVerdictIsTheSameAtStartAndReload`, `dataplane-vpp-ipsec-refuses-start`) are implemented here with their tests of the answered-choice table. Every repository user of `fib kernel` under `vpp` already carries `fib vpp` beside it (searched 2026-11-23: `test/plugin/fib-vpp-coexist-with-fib-kernel.ci` and U-7), so the AC-14 refusal reaches no further user

### Critical Review Checklist
| Check | What to verify for this spec |
|-------|------------------------------|
| Completeness | Every AC-N has an implementation at file:line |
| Feature completeness | Each of A to F, H, J and K has a refusing `.ci` and a surviving `.ci`; B also has its start and its `vpp`-only reload `.ci`; flowspec-firewall has both of its AC-3 files; G has `static-named-table-follows-data-plane` on both planes; C-2 (b), C-3 (a), C-4 (a), C-5 (a), Q-1 (b), Q-2 (a) and P-1 (b) each have their tests in the answered-choice table; P-1 (b) has `TestDataPlaneVerdictRefusesMarkedFIBWriterAlone`, `dataplane-vpp-refuses-fib-kernel-without-fib-vpp` and `dataplane-vpp-fib-kernel-without-fib-vpp-refuses-start`; Q-1 (b) has `dataplane-vpp-accepts-sysctl`; Q-2 (a) has `TestValidateReadsCommandLinePlugins`, `dataplane-validate-sees-command-line-plugin` and, for AC-18, one `.ci` for each daemon door, the pushed candidate and the ephemeral editor, each with a recorded red taken with the `RecordCommandLinePlugins` call removed (A-16; for the ephemeral editor, the forwarded `--plugin` arguments removed); the record has exactly one writer outside tests, the selection function is its one reader that decides, the upload receipt and `startEphemeralDaemon` only display or forward it, and no door, constructor or hook takes a plugin list; P-2 (a) has `firewall-vpp-flow-offload-refused` (AC-17) |
| Correctness | A default backend that nothing uses refuses nothing; every selector default comes from the schema, including the one `iface.BackendNameFromTree` reads from a Go constant today |
| Naming | Plane names, selector values, backend registration names and `ze:backend` values are exactly `kernel` and `vpp` (N-1); no `netlink`, `nft` or `tc` remains as any of them, and no alias accepts one. Go package names and the kernel mechanisms they implement keep their names |
| Data flow | The config component imports no component and names no config path; every path comes from a declaration |
| Operator errors and doctor codes | Every refusal of the verdict is an `opererr` error with its fix on the last line and the code `doctor-dataplane-mismatch`; the verifier refusals (F, H, K) keep their verifier's words; B's tree validator refusal carries the fix `set vpp plugins wireguard true` and its `doctor-vpp-wireguard` code |
| Answered choices | Each of C-2 to C-5, Q-1, Q-2, P-1, P-2 and N-1 is implemented as Thomas answered it, and no config or engine code names a FIB plugin, IPsec or flow export to do it. No start-only path exists: `LoadConfig` has one signature, and no declaration says how a start treats it |
| Rule: `ai/rules/no-layering.md` | No component still calls the gate; the hand page is gone before the generated one exists |
| Rule: converts users | Every row of Repository users the refusals reach is converted or re-validated in the phase its implementation step names, with the `ze-test-judge` approval each test change needs; a user the search missed is added to the table before it is converted |

| Generated page | `./le doc check all` |

### Security Review Checklist
| Check | What to look for |
|-------|-----------------|
| Fail-open verdict | A selector or plane that cannot be read is a refusal, never "no mismatch"; the walker's silent nil on a missing schema node is not carried into the verdict |
| Input validation | The verdict reads only the parsed tree and the registry |
| Error leakage | Refusals name config paths and backend names, no Go symbol |

### Failure Routing

| Failure | Route To |
|---------|----------|
| Compilation error | Fix in the phase that introduced it |
| Test fails for the wrong reason | Fix the test assertion or setup |
| Test fails on behavior mismatch | Re-read the source in Current Behavior. If misunderstood → RESEARCH |
| Lint failure | Fix inline. If architectural → DESIGN |
| Functional test fails | Check the AC: wrong AC → DESIGN, correct AC → IMPLEMENT |
| Audit finds a missing AC | Back to the relevant phase and implement |
| 3 fix attempts failed | STOP. Report all 3 approaches. Ask the user |

## Design Insights

- The firewall YANG annotations are per leaf and the VPP verifier is per value, so the two can disagree by construction (I). The annotation is the gate for operator config; the verifier is the gate for tables other plugins build.
- A table producer's tables reach whichever firewall backend is loaded, and with no `firewall` block that is the OS default: a producer is a firewall user even when the operator wrote no firewall.
- Only copp and the IRR interface bindings build tables whose shape the configuration alone fixes and that can reach `firewall backend vpp` unrefused: the IRR set contents arrive at run time, but the verifier refuses a named set by its shape. Policy routing and GTSM build from config too, but every combination of either with `firewall backend vpp` is already refused by AC-4 or AC-5. A term naming an IRR AS-SET or a domain group is a `MatchInSet` in the firewall's own table, refused at commit today. The others build tables from run-time events and can only be judged when applied.

## Owned journal rows
| Class file | Date | Surface (short) |
|------------|------|-----------------|

## Key Design Decisions
| Decision | Alternatives Considered | Rationale |
|----------|------------------------|-----------|
| Refuse a kernel-only feature under `interface backend vpp` | Host-side declaration shown as implied configuration | Thomas, 2026-11-23 |
| The verdict lives in the config doors (`LoadConfig`, `verifyTree`) | Each component's `OnConfigVerify`; an original-tree validator | `ze config validate` never calls a live `OnConfigVerify`; the tree validator loop stops at the first refusal |
| `interface backend` selects the data plane | A new top-level data-plane leaf | It already selects the FIB writer and PPPoE sessions; a new leaf would be a config syntax change |
| An unused default backend selects nothing | Refuse every VPP config that lacks `firewall backend vpp` | An operator who wrote no firewall and runs no table producer wrote no disagreement |
| Producer tables the configuration fixes are judged by one firewall tree validator, `firewall-producer-tables`, over builders producers register | copp's `InProcessConfigVerifier` calling `firewall.RunVerifier`; one tree validator per producer | An in-process verifier does not run at daemon start (`yang-config-design.md`, Layer 3); one validator per producer is the hand check per plugin set aside, and stops at the first refusal |
| GTSM is refused under `interface backend vpp` | Keep it for the host-side meaning of its kernel writes | Both writers (`installHopLimitRoute`, `publishFilter`) program the kernel only; the decision row forbids a host-side meaning kept to stay accepted |
| The WireGuard plugin rule (B) is an original-tree validator registered by the VPP interface backend | A verifier registry on `iface`, run from the interface component's `InProcessConfigVerifier`; `ConfigReads` of the `vpp` root added to the interface registration; a declaration judged by the data-plane verdict | The interface verifier is handed `interface` and `class-of-service` only and does not run at start, and a `vpp`-only commit would not run it. Adding the `vpp` root to the interface registration's `ConfigReads` would hand the interface component a root it does not own for one leaf, and its verifier would still not run at start. The rule is not a plane mismatch, so it does not belong in the verdict. A tree validator sees the whole tree at every door |
| A producer with no config root is in use when the candidate selects its plugin | A tree predicate in flowspec-firewall spelling the `plugin internal` path; refusing every `vpp` config with no `firewall backend vpp` | The plugin would repeat the config component's reading of the `plugin` block and miss `plugin external` and the command line; the second refuses configurations that use no firewall |
| One verdict, the same at start, reload, commit and validate | A start that reports and withholds the mismatching part; a per-feature start mark for IPsec | Thomas, 2026-11-23: "Refuse invalid configuration at boot", "simpler" |
| `fib kernel` stays accepted under `vpp` beside `fib vpp`, by a mark its own registration sets, and is refused alone, for now | Refusing it outright; loading `fib-vpp` as implied configuration beside it; accepting it alone and leaving VPP's FIB empty | Thomas chose C-3 (a) and P-1 (b), "atm"; `fibPluginChosen` stays |
| All of `sysctl` stays accepted under `vpp`, by the same mark | Refusing it as kernel-only | Thomas chose Q-1 (b): "so we can consider mix setup when we add vrf" |
| Every door judges with the `--plugin` arguments of its process, read from one record the dispatcher writes where it parses them | Recording the gap; accepting the command-line plugin at start; threading the list as a parameter to each door | Thomas, Q-2: "it should start the plugin to get the yang"; one verdict at every door. Threading is set aside in Alternatives set aside: twelve doors, and a door that is handed the list can be handed the wrong one |
| The three firewall annotations are deleted and the verifier decides | Keeping them | Thomas chose C-2 (b) and confirmed P-2 ("p2 delete"); `flow-offload` stays refused by the `flowtable` list's `ze:backend "kernel"` annotation |
| Every data-plane selector names its plane, `kernel` or `vpp`, and the backend's registered name is its plane | Keeping `netlink`, `nft`, `tc`; aliases or a conversion for them; a plane argument beside the name | Thomas, N-1: "so we have vpp and kernel?", "please do it"; pre-release, no layering; one fact declared once |

## Known Limitations
- A VPP router cannot filter its own punted control-plane traffic with kernel nftables (C-1). That is the decided behavior.
- Tables a plugin produces at run time (ddos-local, flowspec-firewall, anomaly shape) are judged when applied (AC-8 survivors).
- The firewall and traffic limit tables stay hand-written (AC-12 survivors).
- `sysctl` stays accepted under `interface backend vpp` (Q-1 (b)), and its keys are applied to the Linux kernel exactly as on any data plane, so a setting about kernel behaviour works unchanged on a VPP box. Only a key about packet forwarding, for example `rp_filter` or `forwarding`, acts on the packets that reach Linux rather than the packets VPP forwards: an `rp_filter` key checks only the packets VPP punts to Linux through the LCP TAP, never the transit traffic VPP forwards (A-1; `plan/journal/silent-fall-through.md`, the `rpf-check` row). The generated page and `docs/guide/vpp.md` say so. That is the decided behavior, kept for mixed setups once VRFs are added.
- A `fib kernel` block with no `fib vpp` block is refused under `interface backend vpp` for now (P-1 (b), "atm"); Thomas may revisit it.
- A box whose stored configuration holds any mismatch this spec refuses, IPsec under `interface backend vpp` included, does not boot after the upgrade that carries this work (C-5 (a), R-8): `ze` exits at start with the refusal and its fix. Before upgrading, the operator runs the new release's `ze config validate` over the stored configuration, as the configuration guide's "Check before you upgrade" shows (`ze config validate /etc/ze/ze.conf`): it gives the refusal start would give, with the same words, and the configuration is fixed before the new daemon starts. The old release's `ze config validate` does not hold the verdict and accepts the file. After the upgrade, `ze doctor` reports the row on the stored file with no daemon running.
- When a running Ze answers `ze config validate` (a file that declares an external plugin is sent to it first, `ai/doors/config-yang.md`), that daemon judges with the `--plugin` list it was started with, not the list on the caller's command line. The answer is then the verdict of the daemon the file would load in. A caller that names other plugins than the running daemon gets no verdict for its own list; the asking route belongs to `spec-explicit-plugin-and-attach-config` (Depends).
- `ze --plugin <name> config edit` with a daemon already running opens a session on that daemon, which judges with the `--plugin` list it was started with, not the editor's command line, as the running Ze that answers `ze config validate` does. Only the ephemeral daemon `ze config edit` starts itself carries the editor's list (AC-18).
- A stored configuration that names `netlink`, `nft` or `tc` as a selector value is refused at start after the upgrade that carries N-1 (R-11): no alias and no conversion exist. The new release's `ze config validate` names each such selector and gives `set <selector> kernel`. A second implementation on one plane, such as eBPF, would take a selector value of its own when it exists: the accepted cost of naming selectors by plane.
- The original-tree validators stop at the first one that refuses, so an operator whose configuration fails both `firewall-producer-tables` and `iface-vpp-wireguard-plugin` sees one, fixes it, and then sees the other. The data-plane verdict joins all of its own refusals.

## Checklist

### Pre-Spec Verification (before the design is presented)
- [ ] Metadata table present, with a valid Status, Depends, Phase and Updated
- [ ] `ai/INDEX.md` keyword table checked
- [ ] An `rfc/short/` summary exists for every RFC referenced
- [ ] Template format followed: the 🧪 emoji, tables rather than prose, `[ ]` never `[x]`
- [ ] No code snippets
- [ ] Files to Modify names feature code, not only tests
- [ ] Current Behavior and Data Flow sections completed
- [ ] AC-N rows carry testable assertions
- [ ] Every assumption has a Basis and a validation method; every failure mode is a risk row
- [ ] Required Reading carries `→ Decision:` / `→ Constraint:` checkpoints
- [ ] Integration Checklist marks "CLI grammar" when a command is added, "Doctor check" when a runtime dependency is

### Goal Gates (MUST pass)
- [ ] AC-1..AC-N all demonstrated
- [ ] Every user story has a working path and a passing test
- [ ] Wiring Test table complete: every row a concrete test name, none deferred
- [ ] The targeted tests for what changed pass, run in the main checkout by the main thread
- [ ] Feature code integrated (`internal/*`, `cmd/*`), not library-only
- [ ] Integration and Documentation checklists answered Yes/No/N-A with evidence
- [ ] Architectural Verification table filled, including registration over hardcoding
- [ ] Critical Review passes, and `ai/rules/quality.md` is satisfied
- [ ] Every A-N confirmed or broken, none `unvalidated`
- [ ] Every item this spec did not do is a spec of its own, named here, in its own bucket

### TDD
- [ ] Tests written
- [ ] Tests FAIL (paste output)
- [ ] Tests PASS (paste output)
- [ ] Boundary tests for all numeric inputs (N-A: the feature takes none)
- [ ] Functional `.ci` tests for end-to-end behavior
- [ ] Interop tests for protocol features (N-A: no wire behavior changes)

### Closure
- [ ] Append `plan/templates/TEMPLATE-CLOSURE.md` and complete every section in it
- [ ] `/ze-review` gate clean, recorded via `internal/le/spec/review.go`
- [ ] Any lesson routed to its governing surface under `ai/rules/planning.md`; no lesson artifact created merely for closure
- [ ] The `Source` cell of the `docs/architecture/core-design.md` design decision row that names this spec is repointed in commit A
- [ ] **Commit A:** code + tests + docs + edited spec + any journal rows owed by the work
- [ ] **Commit B:** `remove plan/release/fixes/spec-dataplane-compatibility-guard.md` only, in the same `./le commit create` script
